#2221. L'ennesimo tormentone del calciomercato: Varela può già valere 40 milioni dopo 10 partite in Italia? Da Piatek a Iturbe, quando il mercato scambia una stagione per una carriera

Nove gol in dieci partite giocate sono certamente un dato. Gustavo Varela è senza ombra di dubbio un giocatore che sta straordinariamente stupendo al suo anno di debutto in Serie A. Ma è tutto 'vero'? E' tutto oro quel che luccica? C'è un assunto da cui partire: il Monza ha trovato il giocatore perfetto per il proprio allenatore, Ivan Juric. E questo è certamente un colpo non da poco, perché la società brianzola, pur nelle mille difficoltà, invischiata nella lotta per non retrocedere, è stata capace di individuare un ragazzo poco noto e conosciuto e capire che le sue qualità e doti erano funzionali per il tecnico. Un po' quello che dovrebbe essere l'obiettivo primario e principale per ogni direttore sportivo.

Ma può, dopo undici partite, avere già una valutazione da 35 o 40 milioni di euro? Il problema non è che Varela valga o non valga 40 milioni. Il problema è che, dopo così poche gare, qualcuno potrebbe essere chiamato a pagare oggi il calciatore che forse diventerà domani. E lo scotto in molti l'hanno già pagato, abbagliati dal clamore mediatico, da quel che appare, viene percepito, ma in fondo non è davvero. Krzysztof Piatek fece 13 gol in 17 partite in Serie A col Genoa, il Milan spese per lui 38 milioni di euro ma venne di lì a non molto ceduto all'Herta Berlino, nonostante 9 reti nei primi sei mesi. Una grande fiammata per un'illusione (straordinaria) collettiva che non ebbe però seguito e continuità. E figuriamoci Juan Manuel Iturbe, che Walter Sabatini strappò alla Juventus: lo prese la Roma, dopo 8 gol in 33 partite con l'Hellas Verona. Segnò poco, giocò male, l'illusione lì fu quella di aver trovato il nuovo genio del calcio italiano. E anche Patrik Schick, che poi in Germania s'è riconfermato grande al Bayer Leverkusen. Lo prese la Roma, dopo un inizio meraviglioso con la Sampdoria. Lì sì che il talento è vero e con continuità, la Roma non seppe individuarne però la funzionalità (al netto di infortuni e adattamento). Altri esempi? Jackson Martinez, strapagato dall'Atletico Madrid dopo le stagioni al Porto. Andy Carroll, fece 11 reti in 18 gare da titolare in Premier League e al Liverpool lo ricordano più per il codino che per i milioni spesi. Timo Werner, 28 reti in Bundesliga, è arrivato il nuovo panzer, macché, il Chelsea spese una fortuna e mai s'è ripetuto. E poi via, da Renato Sanches a Wilfried Bony, quanti sogni spezzati e milioni gonfiati dall'illusione e dall'entusiasmo.

Per capire il possibile prezzo di Varela bisogna separare due cose: le qualità individuali del centravanti e quelle che il gioco di Juric può amplificare. E' il vecchio assunto e teorema del 'con Gian Piero Gasperini all'Atalanta tanti facevano bene e altrove no'. Sapete qual era il segreto? Le scelte della famiglia Percassi e delle dirigenze. La parola 'funzionalità'. Creare un vestito adatto al proprio allenatore, i giocatori giusti al netto di nomi e risonanza mediatica. Varela è un finalizzatore superiore alla media? Oppure sta vivendo un periodo di conversione straordinario? Riuscirebbe a produrre le stesse occasioni in una squadra di vertice? Una big spesso affronta avversari chiusi, che concedono meno profondità. Altra tematica: può fare reparto da solo? Un conto è essere devastante quando il sistema crea spazi e accompagna l'attacco; un altro è diventare il riferimento di una squadra che deve costruire contro una difesa schierata.

Il Monza potrebbe avere trovato in Varela il centravanti ideale per il calcio di Ivan Juric. Ma una cosa è essere il terminale offensivo perfetto di un sistema che esalta verticalità, aggressività e transizioni; un'altra è dimostrare di poter essere il centravanti di una grande squadra, chiamata spesso a scardinare difese schierate. Il mercato rischia di pagare oggi entrambe le versioni, senza sapere ancora quale delle due abbia davvero davanti. In una moda mica solo italiana, beninteso, ma nella quale ci tuffiamo sempre a caso fitto...

#2219. Ex-player has say on Middlesbrough’s promotion chances amid ‘strongest’ team verdict

Middlesbrough are sat joint-top of the Championship table at the moment along with Birmingham City and Stoke City after 15 fixtures. They drew 0-0 away at Watford last time out before the international break.

Kim Hellberg‘s side are back in action this weekend as they aim to return to winning ways after two games without a win. They face Bristol City at the Riverside Stadium.

Ikeme, who had a loan spell at Boro from Wolves back in 2011, has delivered his verdict on his ex-club, as per The Yorkshire Post: “I think they’ll be disappointed if they don’t go up automatically this year but that being said, with Wolves, West Ham and Burnley coming down, it’s going to be tough to get up for anyone. You’ve got the teams in and around the play-off picture, you’ve got the chasing pack as well.”

He added: “Out of the teams that were left in the Championship, they do look like the strongest. I think Wolves will be hoping to kick on as the year goes on but Boro do look like one of the teams that will be in and around it.”

Middlesbrough reached the play-off final in the last campaign. However, they were beaten at Wembley by Hull City.

Boro lacked a proper number nine last term and that was the main reason why they didn’t get into the top two in the end. They have solved that issue with the addition of Will Lankshear from Tottenham Hotspur and he has been prolific.

Lankshear, 21, has scored 15 goals in 16 games in all competitions since his summer switch. He has sat out of their last two matches due to injury against Wrexham and Watford respectively and his team haven’t won without.

Middlesbrough aren’t just a one man team though and have some eye-catching talent at their disposal. Aidan Morris, Sebastian Berhalter and Amario Cozier-Duberry, among others, will all fancy their chances of playing at the top level soon.

Middlesbrough can’t just rely on Lankshear to fire in the goals for them. They need other players like David Strelec and Kyle Joseph to step up to the plate as well.

Strelec has only just returned from injury and could still play a key role over the coming weeks and months. As for Joseph, he has struggled to make an impact since his switch from Hull City in the last transfer window.

The ex-Blackpool man hasn’t scored in the league yet for Hellberg’s side. Boro are having to be patient with him at the moment.

Middlesbrough have an ideal opportunity to return to winning ways this Saturday against Bristol City as they look to get back to the summit. Their upcoming opponents have been in poor form recently and pressure is starting to mount on their head coach Michael Skubala.

#2217. Raffaele De Leonardis: “La Zes, il laghetto, le fake news: tutti i retroscena della trattativa con De Laurentiis per il centro sportivo a Qualiano”

A Radio Napoli Centrale, nel corso di Un Calcio alla Radio è intervenuto Raffaele De Leonardis (sindaco di Qualiano): “Quali sono i progetti da affiancare al centro sportivo del Napoli? Dopo la firma ho già pensato a cosa fare attorno alla zona. Quella sarà una cittadella dello sport, perché affianco ai terreni dove sorgerà il centro sportivo c’è uno stadio, ci sono campi da tennis e abbiamo in animo un palazzetto dello sport e una piscina. Oggi c’è la parte più difficile, quella della burocrazia e infrastrutture. In poco tempo che mi rimane come sindaco mi metterò a disposizione. Quante volte è saltata la trattativa con il Napoli e perché? La prima volta è saltata per il prezzo d’acquisto. Il Presidente De Laurentiis doveva valutare l’investimento. I primi terreni con destinazione urbanistica si parlava di 100 euro a metro quadro. Non c’era bisogno della Zes. Servivano 20 milioni di euro. Non era nelle disponibilità e intenzioni della società. Perché poi bisognava costruire il centro. E comparando con il Viola Park, finanziato dal 20% del Coni, ci fu un primo stop. Successivamente, la Zes paventava di superare vincoli urbanistici, allora si è cominciato a parlare di terreni agricoli. E quindi si è riallacciato il rapporto perché c’erano terreni che rientravano nei desideri della SSC Napoli. C’è stata una visita, sotto la pioggia, il famoso 30 marzo dell’anno dello Scudetto. Il Presidente del Napoli mi invitò allo stadio Maradona, vidi Massimo Boldi, c’era Serena Brancale, c’era De Martino. Quanti vaffa mi sono preso dal Presidente? Abbastanza (sorride ndr). Nell’ultima riunione c’era Chiavelli, c’erano Testa, Discepolo, c’era anche Edo e lì ho capito che forse c’eravamo. Se De Laurentiis si è messo in auto per vedere le distanze dalle stazioni ai terreni? Sì, la prima volta che venne a visitare i terreni prima di Napoli-Milan fece questo percorso in auto. Il Presidente De Laurentiis chiedeva quanto distasse dalla stazione di Afragola, ed erano circa 20 minuti, dalla stazione di Ponte Riccio al centro sono 10 minuti a piedi e da Varcaturo o zone residenziali dei giocatori sono 8 minuti di auto e dallo stadio sono 20 minuti con la tangenziale. Dall’aeroporto ancora meno, 12-13 minuti. De Laurentiis percepiva che la posizione era buona. Fece però un passo indietro in merito alla storia dei rifiuti tossici, si era spaventato, ma non era vero.

Quanto mi hanno dato fastidio le fake news attorno a questo? No, sono il Sindaco di Qualiano, ne sento tante. Però penso che ci sono solo pensieri in negativo. Ma pensiamo anche in positivo. Tutte queste problematiche potrebbero nascere, magari con la Zes. Ma noi che ci guadagniamo? Niente! C’è uno strumento di legge, sono stati fatti i migliori investimenti, noi stiamo sperando che non venga approvato? De Laurentiis ha firmato un atto notarile, un preliminare condizionato e se arriva l’autorizzazione, si trasforma automaticamente in definitivo. Lui che se ne fa di un terreno agricolo!? La Zes ha sette linee guida. Una delle linee riguarda l’attività produttiva per quanto riguarda turismo e sport. Noi non ci opporremo, perché sarebbe da folli. Chiederemo le infrastrutture che dovranno essere realizzate e aiuteremo il Napoli a realizzarle. Noi stiamo rispettando la legge. Se il prof Discepolo presenta il progetto definitivo in 10-15 giorni e partono i 90 giorni della Zes, seguiremo il countdown. Sentivo parlare di un’autorizzazione preliminare per il muro di cinta, con autorizzazione dei proprietari. Se ci sarà un laghetto? Non so se è un vezzo, ma servirà per irrigare i campi. Sarà a mo’ di cisterna. Se ci saranno hotel? Ci saranno sicuramente le stanze per i giocatori. De Laurentiis tiene tanto alla cantera, penso che farà anche un convitto per i giovani”.

I Am Naples Testata Giornalistica - aut. #2216. Lazio, forza del gruppo, Dele-Bashiru: "Quest'anno siamo molto più uniti"

Il centrocampista parla del buon avvio di stagione e del clima nel gruppo.

Fisayo Dele-Bashiru è stato intervistato ai microfoni di Lazio Style Channel al rientro dagli impegni con la Nazionale, in vista della sfida di sabato tra Lazio e Lecce. Nel confronto racconta l'energia portata da Gattuso, la concorrenza interna e la voglia di dare il massimo per la squadra.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Sto bene. Sono felice di essere tornato a Roma. Naturalmente, essere convocato in Nazionale è sempre un momento speciale, soprattutto in occasione delle qualificazioni alla Coppa d'Africa. Le partite sono andate abbastanza bene. La prima è stata positiva, la seconda poteva andare meglio, ma sono felice di essere tornato a Roma. Nel fine settimana ci aspetta una partita importante e non vediamo l'ora di giocarla.

Penso che quest'anno abbiamo iniziato molto bene con Gattuso. Ha portato nuova energia nello spogliatoio e credo che questo si veda nei rapporti tra i compagni, nel nostro rapporto con l'allenatore e anche in quello con i tifosi. Se riusciremo a continuare su questa strada, sarà perfetto.

Tanto, in questo momento c'è molta concorrenza. Quando ho l'opportunità di entrare in campo per aiutare la squadra, cerco sempre di dare il massimo. Se dovessi giocare nel fine settimana, darò come sempre tutto me stesso. Credo che questo sia importante perché, per arrivare lontano in campionato, non bisogna poter contare soltanto sugli undici titolari: anche i giocatori in panchina devono dare il proprio contributo. È fondamentale.

Credo che la concorrenza sia sicuramente positiva e faccia bene. Tutte le migliori squadre del mondo hanno molta competizione interna e penso che questo permetta alla squadra di esprimere al meglio. In questo momento l'atmosfera è molto buona. Credo che nello spogliatoio ci siano molte più battute e momenti di leggerezza rispetto alla scorsa stagione, quando la situazione era differente. Quest'anno siamo molto più uniti e questo contribuisce sicuramente a creare un bel clima all'interno del gruppo.

Da quando sono alla Lazio ho avuto diversi allenatori. Credo che quest'anno un aspetto importante sia il grande coinvolgimento di Gattuso nel lavoro con il gruppo e con ogni singolo giocatore. Ha un ottimo rapporto con tutti i componenti della squadra e penso che questo sia fondamentale. Inoltre, sa perfettamente cosa significhi essere in campo ai massimi livelli, avendo avuto una grande carriera da calciatore. È un allenatore di altissimo livello, tutti i ragazzi gli vogliono bene e dobbiamo continuare a ottenere risultati per lui.

A livello personale sono una persona piuttosto tranquilla. Non lascio che quello che succede all'esterno influenzi troppo il mio modo di essere. Penso di comportarmi in campo proprio come nella vita di tutti i giorni: sono abbastanza rilassato e calmo, anche se so essere aggressivo quando serve. Direi che queste sono le caratteristiche che mi descrivono meglio.

A livello personale, il mio obiettivo è giocare il maggior numero possibile di partite e aiutare la squadra il più possibile, magari con gol e assist. Se riuscirò a fare tutto questo, penso che sarà una stagione davvero molto positiva, non soltanto per me, ma anche per tutti i ragazzi. Adesso dobbiamo semplicemente continuare a lavorare partita dopo partita. Speriamo di riuscire a proseguire su questa buona strada.

#2215. David Dunn predicts Burnley vs Blackburn Rovers scoreline and tells his former club how to hurt their rivals

Blackburn Rovers head to Turf Moor off the back of their 2-1 win at home against West Ham last time out before the international break. Attacking pair Andri Gudjohnsen and Dapo Afolayan got their goals against the Hammers as they secured an eye-catching three points.

The Lancashire outfit are positioned in 13th place in the Championship table after their first 15 fixtures of the season. They are only three points off the play-offs behind both QPR and Southampton.

Dunn, who had two separate spells at Ewood Park during his playing career, has delivered his score prediction ahead of the weekend via The Red Rose Divide on YouTube: “I’m going to go with 2-1 Blackburn. First goal scorer it would be daft not to pick Gudjohnsen but I feel like it might be a set piece. I think there could be some set pieces.”

Blackburn Rovers have seen head coach Tony Mowbray take a step back this week due to health issues. Damien Johnson and Mike Dodds will take first-team duties for the time being.

Dunn believes Blackburn Rovers can hurt Burnley from set-pieces. Centre-backs Hayden Carter and Sean McLoughlin will head up the pitch for set pieces.

In striker Gudjohnsen, Rovers have one of the best attackers in the division at the moment. The 24-year-old, who is an Iceland international with 45 caps to his name, has 11 goals in 15 outings in all competitions in this campaign.

Todd Cantwell has flourished so far this term and he injects valuable quality into the team. He is out of contract next summer and his long-term situation needs addressing sooner rather than later.

Afolayan has also hit the ground running over recent times and can cause problems for opposition defenders. The ex-Bolton Wanderers man joined from St. Pauli last January and initially took a bit of time to find his feet.

Blackburn Rovers will be in confident mood after seeing off West Ham. They can’t afford to be complacent against Burnley though and will need to be at their best to get something, especially in a derby game.

The Clarets were relegated from the Premier League earlier this year after they finished in the bottom three along with the Hammers and Wolves. They have made a poor start to this season and they are in the drop zone along with Preston North End and Derby County.

Fabio Celestini was appointed as their manager in late October after their decision to give Nicky Hayen the sack. He is yet to win in his first three matches, drawing two and losing one.

#2213. Abraham-Frosinone per 4 milioni? L'Hammarby a febbraio ne ha già incassati 12 dal Como

Il trasferimento di un calciatore dal campionato svedese alla Serie A non è certo così inusuale. Da fine anni '90 ad oggi sono 47 i calciatori che hanno lasciato l'Allsvenskan per trasferirsi nel nostro campionato per un ammontare complessivo di oltre 60 milioni di euro. Il 48esimo quasi certamente sarà l'attuale capocannoniere dell'Hammarby Paulos Abraham, calciatore classe 2002 svedese che dopo le ultime partite della stagione 2026 col club di Stoccolma potrebbe trasferirsi in Ciociaria per circa 4 milioni di euro. Ovvero per il valore presente nel suo attuale contratto scadenza 31 dicembre 2028.

Attualmente primo in campionato, l'Hammarby quest'anno si sta giocando il titolo con il Sirius (tra due giorni lo scontro diretto, ndr) dopo aver definito a inizio anno la cessione più importante della storia del club. In pochi però rammentano che quella cessione è stata definita a un club italiano, Ricordate? Trattasi del trasferimento ultimato lo scorso 2 febbraio al Como di Adrian Lahdo, centrocampista classe 2007 che a inizio anno s'è trasferito in riva al Lago per dodici milioni di euro. "E' un centrocampista completo, un giovane di grande talento che ci completa per caratteristiche - raccontava il ds Ludi dopo aver depositato il contratto -. Parliamo di un box to box, che secondo noi è allineato rispetto allo stile di Fabregas. Gli va dato il tempo di adattarsi, ma il nostro ambiente ci permette di inserire giocatori molto velocemente".

In realtà ad oggi l'inserimento sta avvenendo in maniera fin troppo graduale anche perché il livello di competitività nel Como è cresciuto in modo vertiginoso negli ultimi mesi. Lahdo ha fatto il suo esordio per pochi secondi solo a fine ottobre nella sfida vinta 2-0 contro il Sassuolo. Poco, davvero troppo poco per non imporre riflessioni sulla possibilità di cederlo in prestito a gennaio a un club capace di dargli maggiore minutaggio. Del resto, proprio un trasferimento a titolo temporaneo sta permettendo a un altro tesserato del Como arrivato dalla Svezia a inizio anno come Noel Törnqvist - portiere oggi al Monza - di trovare quello spazio che nei primi mesi in riva al Lago non era riuscito a ritagliarsi.

#2212. Morresi, regista della Lazio: nel derby l'assist per il gol di Coppola

Il centrocampista classe 2011 è stato impiegato da Rughetti in tutte le partite della stagione.

Andrea Morresi è il regista del centrocampo della Lazio di Rughetti. Secondo quanto riportato da Gazzetta Regionale, Il classe 2011, arrivato dal Perugia nell’estate del 2025, gioca davanti alla difesa ed è stato impiegato dal tecnico in tutte le partite di questa stagione. Nel derby con la Roma, un suo filtrante ha messo Coppola nelle condizioni di segnare il gol del momentaneo 2-1.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Morresi era approdato nella capitale nella passata stagione, entrando nell’Under 15. Si è preso presto le chiavi della mediana biancoceleste, interpretando il ruolo di playmaker anche nel campionato attuale. La sua presenza è un punto fermo nelle scelte di Rughetti.

Prima del passaggio al Perugia, Morresi è cresciuto nel settore giovanile della Treiese, dai Piccoli Amici ai Pulcini. L’arrivo alla Lazio nell’estate del 2025 ha aperto una nuova tappa del suo percorso: con la maglia biancoceleste ha trovato spazio stabilmente davanti alla difesa.

In quella posizione gestisce il pallone, distribuisce il gioco e cerca gli spazi per i compagni. Alla visione di gioco unisce tecnica e corsa nelle due fasi. Il suo destro si è già fatto notare anche sui calci di punizione, una soluzione con cui può rendersi decisivo.

Il suo contributo non si esaurisce però nella regia arretrata. Quando si apre la possibilità, Morresi avanza verso la trequarti avversaria. È dunque coinvolto sia nella costruzione delle azioni sia nel lavoro che precede le iniziative offensive della squadra.

Fino alla domenica del derby, la Lazio condivideva con la Roma l’imbattibilità in campionato. I biancocelesti si presentavano alla sfida con una struttura difensiva che li rendeva la seconda miglior difesa del raggruppamento. Anche il lavoro di Morresi davanti alla retroguardia contribuisce all’equilibrio della squadra.

Contro i giallorossi, il numero 4 ha giocato in una zona di campo particolarmente affollata. Dall’altra parte c’era il miglior attacco del girone, capace di mettere alla prova la mediana biancoceleste. La Roma ha prevalso per 3-1 dopo aver dominato gran parte degli 80 minuti, ma nella ripresa la Lazio ha trovato la forza di reagire.

L’episodio che ha riaperto momentaneamente la partita è nato dai piedi di Morresi. Un suo passaggio filtrante ha raggiunto Coppola in posizione favorevole; il compagno ha poi mandato il pallone in rete per il 2-1. La speranza biancoceleste si è spenta con il terzo gol romanista, firmato da Abate.

#2211. Multiple Championship clubs interested in January loan deal for highly-rated Leeds United prospect

Championship sides will have eyes firmly on potential targets for the January transfer window. Clubs across the division will be eager to add players who can aid their promotion pushes or survival fights, and one man attracting early attention is Leeds United prospect Jayden Lienou.

Writing on X, Daily Mail reporter Tom Collomosse states that ‘several’ Championship sides are interested in signing the 18-year-old defender on loan. Leeds United are ‘very excited’ about Lienou’s potential, as he has been training with the first team since February and has been involved in Wales’ senior picture too.

Lienou is yet to make his senior debut but has been a regular for the Whites’ U21s after previous spells in the Liverpool, Everton and Manchester City youth setups. He primarily plays as a left-back but has been used as a centre-back in a 3-4-3 as well.

Lienou is a versatile left-sided defender. As said beforehand, he mainly plays as a left-back, but he can drop into a back three as a central defender or move further forward into a wing-back role.

He has had a strong footballing education in a string of top academies, but his performances for Leeds United’s U21s have really announced him as one to watch. It speaks volumes that he has been around the first-team squads for both his club and country.

Lienou has the speed and energy to operate in a flexible role on the left-hand side. His defensive anticipation and strong tackling in one-on-one situations help him adapt to a more defence-focused style as well.

At only 18, he will need time to settle into the senior game and adjust to the pace and power of Championship football. However, his training involvement at Leeds and with Wales means he will have a good idea of the task awaiting him if a loan deal is struck in the winter.

The Championship is an excellent proving ground for young Premier League talents. There have been countless loan success stories over the years, with the 2026/27 season seeing Rio Cardines and Pierce Charles star at Bristol City and QPR respectively, amongst others.

Leeds United will want Lienou to go and play regularly wherever he goes. It means that the club chiefs must carefully weigh up the style of play and existing left-sided defender options for all of those interested in signing him on loan.

At his age, Lienou can benefit from being around a senior squad on matchdays more regularly. He can learn from established senior players at the level and hopefully have a positive impact on whoever he joins.

#2210. Paratici ha 6 mesi per risolvere il rebus portiere ma la Fiorentina il dopo De Gea (forse) ce l'ha in casa

“Niente dura, niente dura, e questo lo sai. Però non ti ci abitui mai”. Parafrasando il poeta, in casa Fiorentina il tempo sembra aver presentato il conto anche a una delle sue certezze più solide: David De Gea. La crisi dello spagnolo continua a tenere banco, alimentando interrogativi che fino a qualche tempo fa sarebbero sembrati impensabili. Paolo Vanoli ha provato più volte a blindarlo, ribadendo la centralità del portiere nello spogliatoio e assicurando che il suo posto non fosse minimamente in discussione. Eppure, le prestazioni raccontano una storia diversa. De Gea sembra essersi progressivamente smarrito, avviluppatosi a quel viale del tramonto al quale, prima o poi, tutti i calciatori si trovano davanti. E a dirlo sono i freddi numeri: seconda peggior difesa con 25 gol subiti, 17,3 di xG (un -7,7 dove solo il Sassuolo fa peggio con -7,9), -3,31 di xGOT (il dato peggiore della Serie A), parate al 59,7% (solo Turati fa peggio tra i portieri con almeno tre presenze). Uno scenario che a Firenze non può lasciare indifferenti e che avrebbe già spinto Fabio Paratici a interrogarsi sulle possibili mosse per il futuro.

L’ipotesi più accreditata vede come portiere del futuro il giovane canterano Tommaso Martinelli. Dopo anni da eterno wannabe, potrebbe essere arrivato il momento in cui al classe 2006 venga concessa la sua chance. L’anno scorso fu uno dei protagonisti della cavalcata alla salvezza della Sampdoria e questa stagione si sta confermando tra i portieri più interessanti di tutta la Serie B con la maglia dell’Avellino. Appena undici gol presi dagli irpini, terza miglior difesa del campionato, con una percentuale di parate per Martinelli che sfiora il 76% e un xGOT da +0,77. L’estremo difensore fiorentino sta mostrando di avere anche tantissima duttilità, abile con il pallone tra i piedi (49% di precisione nei rilanci lunghi, dato migliore di tutta la Serie B) e nell’uscire dai pali (10 interventi fuori area ad allontanare il pallone, dato tra i più alti in Serie B).

Fabio Paratici continua comunque a guardarsi attorno pronto a cogliere ogni opportunità che gli si possa parare davanti. Santiago Beltran del River Plate piace e ha un costo contenuto, visto il contratto in scadenza del dicembre del 2027. Il timore però è che un portiere proveniente dall’Argentina necessiti di un periodo di doveroso adattamento ed è per questo che il ds viola non perde di vista neanche il mercato italiano, dove Elia Caprile resta un profilo apprezzato. Paratici avrà comunque diversi mesi di tempo per sciogliere il rebus portiere, dal momento in cui De Gea non sarà (quasi) certamente messo in discussione a gennaio.

#2208. Juve, Spalletti spera nei recuperi di Nico, Celik e Cambiaso per il Venezia

Da oggi, la Juventus entrerà davvero nel vivo della preparazione in vista della sfida contro il Venezia. Ieri, infatti, è stata una giornata particolare, con Spalletti che ha deciso di suddividere il gruppo in più parti, gestendo il lavoro in base alle condizioni dei singoli giocatori. Da oggi, invece, la squadra sarà al completo e si potrà iniziare a fare il punto della situazione in vista della ripresa del campionato. Sarà soprattutto l’occasione per capire chi potrà recuperare e chi, invece, sarà costretto a dare forfait. La speranza dello staff bianconero è quella di ritrovare alcuni elementi importanti, soprattutto in vista di una partita che può rappresentare un nuovo punto di partenza. Domenica, contro il Venezia, servirà una Juventus concentrata e determinata. Intanto, Spalletti attende risposte importanti dall’infermeria.

Ieri Spalletti ha deciso di dividere il gruppo, gestendo il lavoro dei giocatori in base agli impegni e alle rispettive condizioni fisiche. Infatti, chi durante la pausa è rimasto a Torino ha beneficiato di una giornata di recupero, mentre i nazionali e gli infortunati sono stati al JTC per svolgere programmi personalizzati. Una gestione differenziata, dunque, in attesa di ritrovare tutti gli elementi della rosa. Da oggi la Juventus tornerà a lavorare al completo e sarà possibile avere indicazioni più precise sulle condizioni dei singoli. Per la gara di domenica contro il Venezia, la speranza è quella di recuperare Nico Gonzalez e Cambiaso. Due giocatori che potrebbero rappresentare risorse importanti per Spalletti, considerando le diverse soluzioni che possono offrire alla squadra. La situazione andrà monitorata giorno dopo giorno, senza forzare i tempi. Discorso diverso per Celik, sul quale bisognerà capire se prevarrà la prudenza. Lo staff medico e tecnico non vorrebbe correre rischi inutili, soprattutto per evitare possibili ricadute. La priorità sarà quella di avere a disposizione giocatori nelle migliori condizioni possibili. Le prossime sedute di allenamento saranno quindi decisive per valutare eventuali rientri. Spalletti aspetta risposte dal campo e dall’infermeria, con l’obiettivo di presentarsi alla sfida contro il Venezia con più alternative a disposizione.

La Juventus, domenica, riprenderà il cammino in campionato e le incognite sono tante. Infatti, il gruppo bianconero, da inizio stagione, ha alternato buone prestazioni a partite in cui il gioco latitava. Per questo motivo sarà interessante capire con che spirito ripartirà la squadra di Spalletti. La speranza è che i bianconeri riescano a mostrare la stessa concentrazione vista contro Napoli, AZ Alkmaar e Fiorentina. In quelle occasioni la Juventus ha dato segnali incoraggianti, mostrando un atteggiamento e un’applicazione che dovranno diventare una costante. Non basterà, però, disputare una buona partita: servirà trovare quella continuità che finora è mancata. L’atteggiamento dovrà essere quello giusto fin dal primo minuto, senza sottovalutare l’avversario e senza concedere pause durante la gara. Con questo gruppo, tuttavia, certezze non ce ne sono ancora. Le prestazioni altalenanti delle ultime settimane impongono prudenza e rendono difficile fare previsioni. Spalletti dovrà lavorare soprattutto sulla mentalità, cercando di dare alla squadra un’identità precisa. Il Venezia rappresenterà quindi un test importante anche sotto questo aspetto. I tre punti saranno fondamentali, ma altrettanto importante sarà la prestazione. La Juventus ha bisogno di dimostrare di aver imboccato finalmente la strada giusta. Solo il campo potrà dire se i bianconeri saranno riusciti a trovare quella continuità tanto attesa.

#2207. Middlesbrough, West Ham and Southampton trio wanted by Bundesliga side

The Bundesliga side are said to be keen on the Championship trio ahead of the January transfer window. They are positioned in 8th place in the table after their first nine fixtures and they have 12 points on the board.

Morris, 25, joined Middlesbrough back in 2024, whilst Jander, 23, linked up with Southampton last year following their relegation from the Premier League. As for Engels, 23, he was snapped up by West Ham in the last transfer window after catching the eye up in Scotland at Celtic.

As per Bild, all three players are believed to be on Stuttgart’s radar as they look to bolster their options in the middle of the park. They risk losing key man Angelo Stiller in the near future so they will need to find a replacement for him in the midfield.

Middlesbrough, West Ham and Southampton are all eyeing promotion from the EFL this season if possible. Boro are joint-top of the table at the moment after 15 games along with Birmingham City and Stoke City after they lost in the play-off final at Wembley to Hull City back in May.

Middlesbrough won’t want to lose Morris, especially mid-way through this campaign. He is a key player for Boro and has stepped up since Hayden Hackney’s exit to Everton.

Morris, who is a USA international with 14 caps to his name, has made 98 appearances since his switch to the Riverside Stadium from Columbus Crew. Kim Hellberg’s side will face a battle to keep hold of him in the long run though, especially if they don’t go up this term.

West Ham landed Engels to boost their midfield department after they slipped out of the top flight earlier this year. The Belgium international has played in the German top flight before with FC Augsburg before Celtic came calling.

Southampton ace Jander was at Borussia Dortmund and Schalke as a teenager. He then made his name at MSV Duisburg before his previous club 1. FC Nürnberg swooped in for his signature.

Middlesbrough, West Ham or Southampton don’t need to sell anyone in January. They could all sabotage their promotion pushes if they cash in on any of their most prized assets.

Stuttgart will need to pay big money to get any of Morris, Engels of Jander anytime soon. They will probably need to wait until next summer as things stand.

Boro face Bristol City at home on Saturday, whilst the Hammers play struggling Preston North End at the London Stadium. Meanwhile, the Saints lock horns with Derby County at St Mary’s.

#2205. Welshman and Premier League coach among two early contenders to replace Vitor Matos as Swansea City boss

Swansea City saw head coach Vitor Matos’ departure confirmed on Wednesday. The Portuguese boss has left to take charge of RC Strasbourg, with the Swans receiving a compensation fee of around £5m and placing Leon Britton, Kris O’Leary and potentially Ashley Williams in charge on an interim basis.

The hunt for Matos’ replacement is on, and talkSPORT reporter Alex Crook states Welsh boss Rob Edwards and Liverpool first-team coach Tommy Elphick are contenders. The fresh news comes after reputable fanzine the Swansea Independent stated early talks with Edwards were held, and Elphick would be ‘open to discussions’.

Edwards is out of work and would not require compensation. Elphick’s role in Andoni Iraola’s backroom staff at Anfield means the Swans would have to pay a fee to bring him to South Wales if they wish.

Edwards and Elphick are two intriguing candidates in Swansea City’s search for a boss.

Of the two, Edwards is the most obvious. The Welshman is experienced at Championship level from his spells with Watford, Luton Town and Middlesbrough, and he has promotion pedigree to his name from his time with the Hatters. He had Boro flying too, before an ultimately ill-fated move to Wolves.

Like Matos, he favours high-intensity football, and his use of wide players at Luton Town could see him use the Swans’ squad effectively.

At the other end of the spectrum, Elphick has no senior management experience. He has emerged as a highly regarded coach since retiring in 2021, starting out in the Bournemouth youth setup before stepping into the senior side with Gary O’Neill and retaining his role after Andoni Iraola’s arrival.

Iraola took him to Liverpool with him in the summer. The fact he has never been the lead man in the dugout means we can’t know what to expect from his tactical plan, but if his principles are aligned with Iraola’s, expect fast ball progression and aggressive pressing.

Swansea City will be frustrated to lose another manager, just as they started to really build to something special. The same thing happened when Russell Martin was poached by Southampton.

The Swans made great strides under Matos’ lead, so ideally they need a head coach who will oversee a similar tactical plan. It will help them work well with the existing squad and should limit teething problems after an unforeseen change in the dugout.

Swansea City will be carefully weighing up a number of candidates but there will be an eagerness to replace Matos as soon and efficiently as possible to limit how much it disrupts their season.

Palestra with a loose first touch but he recovers and defends well. Like in Sydney, this looks another very bumpy pitch, you can see the join marks between the pieces of turf that have been freshly laid. Coming out of these games without any injuries will be a priority for Alonso.

Juventus XI: Di Gregorio, Kalulu, Gatti, Kelly, Cambiaso, Douglas Luiz, Locatelli, Miretti, Boga, Milik, Celik

Subs: Perrin, Pinsoglio, Nava, Bremmer, Rugani, Koopmeiners, Arthur Melo, Cabal, Owusu, Gil Puche, Oboavwoduo, Licina, Durmisi, Yildiz, Zhegrova, David

Welbeck starts against Juventus, but no Jordan Henderson with the group just yet. As Sam Dean writes here: “Last season, especially, provided evidence that the balance of their squad was not right, and they are now trying to change the internal dynamic by adding more leadership and experience to the team.”

How long will he stay there? Will he play his way back into Alonso’s team, be shipped out on loan or sold for a fee? Sam Dean assesses the options for player and club in this piece. Out of sight, out of mind could well be the most likely outcome for a player who has missed almost two years of football, and looked extremely raw before his ban.

Looks like a back four once again for Alonso, and Danny Welbeck makes his first Chelsea appearance. Pedro will play off Welbeck as a second striker. Geovany Quenda was signed last year from Sporting CP, but joined up with Chelsea this summer. Another wide forward in their collection.

The wheels of commerce continue to turn for Chelsea in the transfer market but this afternoon Xabi Alonso’s team continue their preparations for the new season against Juventus in Hong Kong.

They have the chance to recover from the ignominy of losing to 10-man Tottenham Hotspur in Sydney on Saturday, but with 19 days until Chelsea’s Premier League opener against Fulham these matches are still a fitness building exercise. The positive from the Spurs game was Wesley Fofana, Levi Colwill, Cole Palmer completing 80 minutes – three players who endured injury problems last season.

Another new arrival is expected to follow Morgan Rogers, Marco Palesta, Maxence Lacroix, Jordan Henderson and Danny Welbeck to Stamford Bridge, with the club nearing a deal for Rayo Vallecano left-back Pep Chavarria.

It wouldn’t be Chelsea without talk of some eye-catching outgoings, with Spurs reportedly interested in Nicolas Jackson and Enzo Maresca eyeing a reunion with Pedro Neto at Manchester City.

Alonso’s team promise to be flexible in their tactical approach this season, but so far he has set up with a back four rather than the 3-4-2-1 system he used to such great effect at Bayer Leverkusen. That could change as availability increases closer to the start of the season, but Palestra’s bursts forward from right-back in tandem with Estevao have caught the eye.

Juventus finished sixth in Serie A last season under Luciano Spalletti, the former Roma and Napoli coach who usually guarantees expansive and attractive football. Turkey left-winger Kenan Yildiz was their top goalscorer last season, and it looks like they will make it through the summer without the left-winger being picked off by one of the Premier League’s elite. Yildiz scored 11 goals in all competitions, and Dusan Vlahovic was the only other player to reach double figures so scoring goals is a problem to address. Lois Openda and Randal Kolo Muani have been signed this summer to strengthen the forward line.

#2203. Godspeed, la storia di Djed Spence che della velocità ne ha fatto religione

«Faccio tutto alla velocità dettata da Dio. Qualunque essa sia, lenta o veloce. Lascio che sia Lui a guidarmi». Djed Spence ha scelto queste parole per spiegare il significato di Godspeed, lo slogan che accompagna i suoi profili social e che porta anche al polso, come un promemoria personale. Una dichiarazione di fede, prima ancora che un motto. Ma c'è qualcosa di curioso nel modo in cui questa filosofia sembra incrociare il suo calcio. Perché quando l'inglese parte sulla fascia, il tempo degli avversari sembra improvvisamente rallentare e quello dei tifosi nerazzurri, invece, accelerare. Spence sta vivendo una di quelle fasi in cui un giocatore smette di essere una novità e comincia a diventare un'attesa. Non basta più guardarlo per capire cosa possa dare. Si comincia ad aspettare che giochi, che punti l'uomo, che strappi, che accenda lo stadio. È successo a Rotterdam, il 3 novembre, nella notte del 3-0 contro il Feyenoord. Novanta minuti da protagonista, l'assist per Bisseck, il cross deviato che ha propiziato il raddoppio di Esposito e il riconoscimento di MVP assegnato dalla UEFA. Una prestazione che ha messo in mostra velocità, personalità e capacità di incidere su entrambe le fasi. Quattro giorni più tardi, contro il Como, un'altra prova da titolare, altri novanta minuti e un assist nella vittoria per 2-0. Due partite, due segnali. La sensazione è che l'Inter vedova di Dumfries abbia trovato un giocatore capace di trasformare una corsa in un'occasione e una corsia in un territorio da conquistare.

I numeri analizzabili fino a questo momento restituiscono la fotografia di un inserimento che sta diventando sempre più concreto. Sette presenze complessive tra Serie A e Champions League, 362 minuti giocati e due assist. Nel dettaglio, quattro apparizioni in campionato per 201 minuti e tre in Europa per 161 minuti. Nessun gol, almeno per ora, ma già due partecipazioni decisive alla produzione offensiva nerazzurra. Interessanti anche i dati delle ultime due uscite: contro il Feyenoord ha completato 43 passaggi su 48, contro il Como 42 su 48. In entrambe le occasioni ha disputato l'intera partita, confermando una continuità che va oltre la giocata spettacolare. Eppure sarebbe riduttivo raccontarlo soltanto attraverso le statistiche. La sua caratteristica più evidente è la capacità di rompere gli equilibri. Conduzione, dribbling, fisicità e una duttilità che gli permette di interpretare più ruoli sulla corsia. Non a caso, nell'intervista di presentazione ai canali nerazzurri, ha indicato proprio nel dribbling e nel portare il pallone in avanti le qualità che meglio lo rappresentano, individuando nella duttilità il suo punto di forza. C'è anche una storia che si intreccia con il suo presente milanese. Il 4 marzo 2024, quando vestiva la maglia del Genoa, aveva già assaggiato San Siro, giocando i suoi primi 28 minuti nello stadio contro l'Inter. Oggi quello stesso prato è diventato casa sua. E il numero 99 sulle spalle, nato dall'unione del giorno della sua nascita, il 9, con il numero preferito del padre, aggiunge un dettaglio personale a un'avventura che sta assumendo contorni sempre più importanti.

C'è un aspetto, però, che rende Spence qualcosa di più di un esterno capace di accendere una partita. È il rapporto che sta costruendo con l'ambiente. Il suo modo di stare in campo, l'energia, la disponibilità al sacrificio e quella naturalezza con cui affronta l'uno contro uno stanno conquistando una tifoseria che ama riconoscersi nei giocatori capaci di non risparmiarsi. Lui stesso, raccontandosi, si è definito divertente, tranquillo e sicuro di sé. Tre caratteristiche che sembrano convivere anche nel suo modo di giocare. Leggerezza nella conduzione, tranquillità nelle scelte e sicurezza quando c'è da puntare l'avversario. La fede, poi, non è una parola utilizzata per costruire un'immagine. L'inglese ha raccontato di essersi avvicinato personalmente a questa filosofia e di affidarsi a Dio anche nella gestione della pressione, attraverso la preghiera. È un tratto autentico della sua identità, che aiuta a comprendere meglio il significato di quel Godspeed ripetuto sui social. L'Inter gli ha dato una nuova occasione, lui sta rispondendo con prestazioni che alimentano entusiasmo e aspettative. La strada è ancora lunga, perché sette presenze e due assist non bastano per scrivere una storia. Ma certi legami cominciano prima dei traguardi, nascono da una corsa, da un contrasto, da un pallone recuperato quando sembra ormai perso. Spence ha cominciato a prendersi la fascia, poi la fiducia dell'allenatore e infine quella dei tifosi. Adesso San Siro aspetta di vederlo ancora sfrecciare. Godspeed, Djed. L'Inter ha trovato la sua velocità.

#2202. Significant Leicester City ownership update emerges after being listed for £200m in August

Leicester City have had financial problems for some time now, and discontent towards long-term owners King Power has grown amid the club’s 10-year slide from Premier League champions to League One. As a result of growing unrest and dire finances, LCFC were put up for sale in August.

Now though, after limited progress and minimal news, The Telegraph has reported that the Foxes are no longer up for sale. Senior staff in Leicestershire have been informed by the Thai leadership that the club is off the market with the full focus on winning League One promotion.

Despite their debt, the club were valued at £200m upon being listed for sale. This valuation and the owners’ refusal to lower their asking price has proven a major stumbling block, and now there will be no takeover at the King Power Stadium.

Chairman Aiyawatt Srivaddhanaprabha was reluctant to list Leicester City for sale in the first place. He and the King Power ownership have consistently expressed a desire to lead the club back from their poor position and to their previous heights.

However, being put up for sale and entertaining takeover bids indicated that the ownership had realised they had done all they could to try and salvage the club to no avail. But, with a £200m valuation and tremendous debts, LCFC have proven a hard sell – despite their modern training ground and stature.

It means the supporters will have to stick it out with the current hierarchy for some time yet. The chairman and his close affiliate Jon Rudkin have been heavily scrutinised in recent years, but despite a shuffle in the boardroom and the appointment of James McCarron as sporting director, much of the day-to-day responsibility still lays with ‘Top’ and Rudkin.

No takeover means no new money for the Foxes. The financial hole they have dug for themselves will only get deeper.

As of the most recently published accounts, Leicester City have averaged losses of £60m per year for the past three seasons. Past accounts showed their debt sat at a monstrous £163m.

Now, LCFC are without the Premier League riches, and revenue on the whole will decrease drastically while they stay in League One. To find some sort of salvation without a takeover, the Foxes simply have to be successful on the pitch.

An extended stint in League One will only see them burn a deeper hole in their pockets, and it will be increasingly more difficult to clamber out of it.

#2201. ​Arteta’s ‘dynasty’ dream collides with Arsenal’s harsh reality

Mikel Arteta says he wants to create a “dynasty” at Arsenal as he threw down the gauntlet to his players to retain their Premier League title and reach a “different level”.

Arsenal are on the verge of completing a £75m deal for Brazil midfielder Bruno Guimãraes from Newcastle and have not given up hope of landing Real Madrid winger Vinícius Júnior, who has been offered an improved contract by the Spanish giants.

The Premier League champions slumped to a 3-1 defeat to Real Betis in Dublin on Wednesday night, when they paid the price for individual errors and a lethargic, sloppy defensive display.

But that did little to quell Arteta’s ambitions for the new season. “I’m even more excited [about this season], and we’re going to go and attack every possible title because that’s the ambition that we have,” he said.

“We know that we have the ability. We will go from a strength to a strength as well, whether it’s with the players that we have to take them to a different level, and with the market that is still open to recruit as well, so we are very optimistic about it.

“All the work that we’ve done – that I’ve done through the summer – is to understand what we need to do to win again and to create a dynasty in this football club. That’s the aim.”

Despite Arteta’s bullishness, he conceded Arsenal would have to be a lot better than they were against Betis. When asked about the mood in the dressing room, he said: “Very upset, given a hard time. They are fuming, but this is good. That’s what we need.”

Telegraph Sport analyses some of the talking points from a disappointing evening at the Aviva Stadium.

The importance of goalkeeper David Raya and centre-backs William Saliba and Gabriel Magalhães to Arsenal hardly needs overstating. Their title success was built on the foundations provided by that triumvirate at the back.

Raya has yet to report back from holiday after Spain’s World Cup triumph and Saliba is facing an extended spell on the sidelines with a back injury. Gabriel did appear for the second half but, watching this, Arteta is unlikely to have been dissuaded from the belief he needs another top defender to compensate for the loss of Saliba.

It may have been only Arsenal’s second game of pre-season, so a degree of legginess was understandable.

But the ease with which they were cut open and overloaded by the Spanish side, and the volume of individual errors, did not sit comfortably with Arteta with just over a fortnight until they kick off their title defence at home to newly promoted Coventry City.

Only once in 63 matches last season did Arsenal concede three goals in a single game but they were fortunate Betis did not score more. “In the first half there are two different things: to play well and to compete,” said Arteta, who saw his side trail 3-1 at the interval.

“We did the first one quite well in a lot of moments but the compete part – especially in and around our box – was not to the standards to win a football match against a good side. It’s better to give them [the goals away] in a friendly match than an official match. It’s always good when you lose a match in pre-season because it creates a bit more fire in the belly.”

Kepa Arrizabalaga will not want to watch Betis’s first goal back in a hurry. The Arsenal goalkeeper may have got a slight nudge as he came for the ball at a corner but he was all over the place and never looked even in the vaguest control of the situation. Rodrigo Riquelme scored at the far post after the ball sailed over the keeper. Arteta called it a “really sloppy goal”. With former Leeds goalkeeper Illan Meslier having arrived on a free transfer as back-up to Raya, Kepa faces an uncertain future at Arsenal. Moments like this will not help his cause.

He did not feature much last season but is probably best remembered for his torrid showing in the Carabao Cup final defeat by Manchester City in March. He looked as unconvincing here as he did then, as the former Chelsea goalkeeper also sliced a clearance in the build up to Betis’s third goal. Before then, Kai Havertz had turned over possession cheaply from which Nelson Deossa set up former West Ham midfielder Pablo Fornals to score.

Arsenal need more creativity and firepower in attack, hence the interest in Vinícius and Atlético Madrid’s Julián Alvarez. Viktor Gyokeres again looked rather limited here and the likes of Bukayo Saka and Eberechi Eze are also still to report back. Yet one of the few, brighter aspects of Arsenal’s performances was the sight of the regal looking Riccardo Calafiori marauding forward from a very nominal left-back role.

If Arsenal are going to play more adventurously this season and try to ratchet up things in the entertainment stakes then Calafiori could have a big role to play, as long as Arteta can keep him fit. He can be a potent weapon galloping forward and there were clear glimpses of that here. Harnessing that talent while not compromising too much defensive security may be one of the manager’s challenges.

Louie Copley had only been on the pitch for nine minutes – a half-time substitute for Ben White – when the 19-year-old midfielder went down largely unchallenged with what looked like a troubling knee injury. Arteta admitted afterwards that the way the teenager fell to the ground was a concern and he looked in visible pain and distress as he was treated by the medical staff before being helped off the pitch.

The fear will be of some kind of ligament damage and Arteta confirmed Copley would be scanned on Thursday. “Quite worried to be fair for the kid because the mechanism doesn’t look very positive and the symptoms that he already has,” the manager said. “So we are going to have to wait and see for tomorrow’s scan. Touch wood that is not a bad one.”

We are taking steps in the right direction. Of course, we want more from the performance and the results, but we take learnings and move on.

We are so grateful for everyone who came to support us today. We wanted to give them a good result and performance. Hopefully next time we can.”

I want to be myself and to show on the pitch all the passion and desire to win because I really like to win games and to give everything.

“If I give 100% for every game and every duel, I think who does not like this? I think that is the best thing for a football player, and then you will always have the support from the supporters. I am thinking about how to improve and settle as fast as possible in the club, because I have the opportunity to train directly with the team from the beginning of the season, to play pre-season games and then to go to official games. This is a chance to play directly from the beginning and then it is up to me to show the qualities and what I can bring to the team. I am pretty confident I will show it in the next games.”

Real Betis have already played a number of friendlies as they build up to the start of their season, which begins on August 21 when they take on Real Sociedad.

#2200. Out-of-form West Ham man set to keep starting berth vs Preston North End with potential replacement ‘struggling’

West Ham goalkeeper Mads Hermansen has been under fire recently, with poor performances prompting calls for Alphonse Areola to be brought into the starting XI. However, the Frenchman suffered an injury in the warm-up before WHUFC’s defeat to Blackburn Rovers before the break, ruling him out.

And, after the international break, doubts remain over Areola’s availability. Speaking to the official club media ahead of Saturday’s clash with Preston North End, Scott Parker said: “We’re struggling a bit with Alphonse at the moment. He probably looks a bit unlikely. We’ll probably leave it as late as we can to see if he’s fit to be involved.”

Elsewhere, Max Kilman is suspended, but Kyle Walker-Peters could be in line for a return. Parker made no mention of the full-back in his pre-match interview, saying: “There’s nothing really jumping out, other than Alphonse.”

Areola isn’t ruled out of West Ham’s clash with Preston North End, but it would be a surprise to see him start. If Parker goes into the weekend planning to start the Frenchman and it transpires that he isn’t ready to go on matchday, it throws that section of the pre-match preparations out of the window.

Despite his struggle for form, it is smarter to prepare for the game with Hermansen in goal. That will frustrate some sections of the WHUFC support, but it is the sensible move to make.

Hermansen has started every Championship game this season, but his performances have been far from convincing.

He isn’t solely responsible for the underwhelming defensive record of four clean sheets and 24 goals conceded in 16 games across all competitions. However, the Dane’s displays have left a lot to be desired with an inability to command his box and errors when coming to claim crosses leading to chances for the opposition.

Given his form, a fully fit Areola may have got the nod. For this weekend though, it seems Hermansen will keep his place.

When Areola is at 100%, Hermansen will have a fight on his hands to keep his place.

It means that if he does start again against Preston North End as expected, he needs to put in a far more assured performance. A clean sheet would not go amiss, but the most important thing is to act with confidence and conviction. If he can command his box, comfortably claim crosses and show composure on the ball, it will go a long way to easing concerns around his form.

If not, Areola will be waiting in the wings to come into the WHUFC side when fit. If his absence extends longer, then youngster Finlay Herrick may be considered if Parker feels a chance is needed.

#2199. Liverpool coach joins Rob Edwards on Swansea City manager radar

Swansea City are on the hunt for a new head coach after Vitor Matos departed for Ligue 1 side Strasbourg, and both Rob Edwards and Tommy Elphick are among the contenders to replace him.

The Championship side has yet to formally announce Matos' departure, but the BlueCo-led French outfit confirmed his arrival on Wednesday, with the Swans set to receive around £5 million in compensation for his services.

Matos leaves Swansea having taken them from 20th in the second tier upon his arrival midway through last season all the way to the summit in the early stages of the ongoing campaign.

A run of four defeats in five leading up to the November international break saw them drop to sixth, but they're still three points off Birmingham City, who currently occupy the top spot.

Now, Swansea have to source someone who can maintain their strong start, as they look to mount a promotion challenge after five consecutive campaigns finishing between 10th and 15th.

According to TalkSPORT's Alex Crook, two of the early candidates to replace Vitor Matos as Swansea boss are former Wolves head coach Rob Edwards and Liverpool coach Tommy Elphick.

Edwards has been out of work since the summer, when he was rather unceremoniously sacked by Wolves following their relegation from the Premier League. He found out the news from social media while he was on holiday, after being one of the driving forces behind the signing of Kieran Trippier and the return of Raul Jimenez.

Which fanbases can improve on their current atmosphere offerings? The computer has had its say....

In the Championship, he has experience at the top end of the table. He took Luton Town up into the Premier League in the 2022/23 campaign, and last season had Middlesbrough battling amongst the automatic promotion places before leaving for Molineux.

He has had his failings at this level, though, such as with Luton following their relegation from the top flight and at Watford for a brief period in 2022, although he isn't the only manager to fall victim to the events at Vicarage Road.

Meanwhile, this isn't the first time that Elphick has been linked with a head coach role at Championship level. The former Bournemouth defender rejected the opportunity to take over at Bristol City during their managerial search during the off-season, instead following Andoni Iraola to form part of his coaching staff at Liverpool.

The 39-year-old has coached ever since his retirement in 2021, being part of the first-team staff at the Vitality Stadium since Gary O'Neil's time at the club in 2022, until he left for Anfield in the summer.

Swansea fans are still stung by Matos' departure and the haste with which it happened. Strasbourg's official approach only took place on Monday, with this whole ordeal coming quite out of the blue.

Those at the Swansea.com Stadium had become admirers of the football being played under the Portuguese boss, playing high-intensity, pressing football while also being good in possession, too. Replacing him will be difficult, and supporters online aren't enamoured by the reports linking their post with Edwards and Elphick.

The majority of the discourse is coming Edwards' way, though.

The 43-year-old isn't seen as an inspiring choice, even despite his relative success at Championship level, and some Swans fans don't think he'd be able to oversee a smooth transition from the football seen under Matos.

"Don’t particularly want us to go down the Rob Edwards route to be honest, as I’m not convinced he’s the right fit for us in terms of style of play," one fan said in response to the reports.

"If it is Rob Edwards I'd be extremely disappointed. Would undo all the good work we've done over the last year," another said, while a third commented, "It’s just so underwhelming, isn’t it?"

Even one fan commented, "Rob Edwards, no, Tommy Elphick, yes," indicating that they'd rather have someone who hasn't been a head coach yet in his career over Edwards at this stage of the season.

Swansea went against the grain and took a risk with hiring Matos back in November last year. The then-37-year-old was incredibly inexperienced as a main dugout man, taking charge of 12 games for Portuguese side Maritimo before heading to South Wales.

Especially with the club being advised by Jamestown Analytics now, the data company founded by Brighton and Hove Albion owner Tony Bloom, perhaps it should be expected that Matos' replacement will equally be out of left-field, if the data can advise manager hires, that is.

Elphick seems destined to become a head coach someday, with him learning under one of the best up-and-coming bosses in the English game right now, in Iraola, and his time at Liverpool will expose him to coaching better players against elite opposition both domestically and in Europe.

Who do you think Swansea City will hire to replace Vitor Matos? Let us know in the comments.

It's also worth noting that there's an easy comparison between Matos and Elphick, not just in their relative managerial inexperience, but also in the fact that they've spent time at Anfield in the dugout, with the now-Strasbourg head coach learning under Jürgen Klopp for nearly five years.

It will be interesting to see where Swansea go from Matos; whether they'll hire someone more well-known who could maintain play-off form, or if they bring in someone to help grow with them, like they did this time last year.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2198. Martyn Waghorn makes controversial Richard Keogh claim that some Derby County fans aren't buying

Martyn Waghorn believes the Derby County senior leaders could have done more to prevent the car crash that left captain Richard Keogh injured during his time at the club.

The incident occurred in September 2019, following a Derby County team-bonding evening which began with bowling and continued at the Joiners Arms pub. A small group, including captain Richard Keogh, stayed drinking into the night, per The Guardian.

Tom Lawrence subsequently crashed his Range Rover into the back of Mason Bennett's Mercedes before hitting a lamppost, with Keogh unconscious in the back. Both Lawrence and Bennett were over the drink-drive limit and initially left the scene. Keogh suffered serious knee ligament damage, facing around 15 months out.

According to the report in The Guardian, the controversy deepened when Derby sacked Keogh for gross misconduct, although he later won £2.3 million in compensation.

However, Martyn Waghorn believes more could have been done to prevent the incident altogether during his interview with BBC Sport Derby, stating: "We can't get away from the fact that it's completely unacceptable.

"I don't know the full ins and outs, [but he (Richard Keogh) was scapegoated, wasn't he?] Yeah, he was.This is really difficult for me.

What do you make of Waghorn's statement? Comment below!

"He shouldn't have been sacked the way he was so abruptly, but I understand the decision. In the long-term, as a senior leader, I think he could have had a bit more of a say in terms of no, let's not do this.

"You're the captain of the football club. Taxis are not far away. We can easily sort something out. I just feel some senior figures could have done more to prevent the situation and it was easily preventable."

Waghorn's statement won't impress everyone but he will mostly be fondly remembered at the club, having enjoyed two spells at Derby, scoring 37 goals in 150 appearances.

He initially joined from Ipswich Town in 2018 after an impressive season, having previously enjoyed a prolific spell at Rangers, where he scored 44 goals in 78 appearances and won the Scottish Championship.

Waghorn was part of Frank Lampard's side that reached the 2019 play-off final and later scored a crucial penalty against Sheffield Wednesday in May 2021 to help Derby avoid relegation.

He returned in 2023, scoring seven goals as Paul Warne's side won promotion from League One. Having done that, he retired from professional football in 2025 after spells at Northampton and Coventry City.

Not everyone connected with Derby County has reacted well to the statement, with one Derby supporter on X stating: "Just another grown man not taking accountability for his actions, nothing new."

Another Derby fan said: "Imagine he actually took accountability"

The 25 top scorers in the Championship since the second tier was rebranded back in 2004/05, including Billy Sharp and Aleksandar Mitrovic.

A further reply on X read: "Grown men should have known better plain and simple. Especially when the club captain is involved. Don’t get why post this when things are still a bit fractious at the club at the minute. Poor from you. @BBCDerbySport #dcfc #dcfcfans"

Another reply from an angry Derby fan added: "They’re adults earning thousands of pounds a week, do you really need your captain to book you a taxi?"

#2197. Positive Sheffield Wednesday injury update emerges with 75 mins per goal star fit for Barnsley clash

Sheffield Wednesday are back in action on Friday as they face Yorkshire rivals Barnsley at Oakwell. Dave Challinor will be eager to build on his first SWFC win against Plymouth Argyle, and he’ll be helped by an improving injury picture at Hillsborough.

Speaking to the press ahead of Friday’s game, Challinor revealed that star forward Louie Barry is fit for the clash with the Tykes after making his return off the bench against Plymouth. The 23-year-old has come through another week of training scot-free, but the boss remains wary that he needs to be managed carefully after his long-term ankle injury.

Elsewhere, Ashley Maynard-Brewer and Ali Al-Hamadi have trained with no issues, and internationals Kieran Morrison, Jordi Liongola, Sean Fusire and Will Grainger returned with no injuries. With no new blows, the Owls only remain without Max Lowe, Reece James, Mason Burstow and Tyler Onyango.

Sheffield Wednesday have lacked a killer instinct in attack at times this season. The failure to convert control into goals played a part in Henrik Pedersen’s downfall, so it is down to Challinor to right the ship.

The 3-0 win over Plymouth Argyle showed big attacking improvements, and their frontline only grows stronger with Barry’s return. He is a proven star at League One level and has already shown SWFC fans how potent he can be in his limited time on the pitch since joining in the summer.

Barry’s comeback will give Challinor a welcome but tricky selection dilemma to resolve. The former Aston Villa and Barcelona prospect can play at striker or on the left-hand side, and there is strong competition for both of these positions.

Davis Keillor-Dunn‘s form has seen him solidify a place in the team, while it was Callum Slattery and Barry Bannan who played in the most advanced roles behind the number nine against Plymouth Argyle.

If Challinor is to play with a three-man frontline, Barry may be perfect for the place on the left. However, the Owls boss will have to work out who drops from the team to facilitate his inclusion.

There is an understandable eagerness to see Barry back in the first XI. He is one of League One’s standout players and has the potential to be a real star in an exciting chapter at Sheffield Wednesday.

However, Challinor is right in saying that he can only play such a role if they are smart with his return. If they rush him into too much too soon, he will be at risk of re-injury or separate setbacks.

By managing his minutes over the coming weeks, SWFC give themselves the best chance of having Barry fit and available for longer. He will be a key influence in Challinor’s attack, but only if they’re careful in the early days of his comeback.

#2196. Gomes scores for Aston Villa in defeat by Bayern Munich in Hong Kong

Aston Villa’s tour of the Far East ended in defeat as they lost 2-1 to German champions Bayern Munich in a pre-season friendly at the Kai Tak Sports Park in Hong Kong on Friday.

Bayern took the lead inside the final 10 minutes of the first half, with defender Kim Min-jae heading in from Tom Bischof’s sublime free-kick. Villa goalkeeper Marco Bizot made a string of saves in the second half to keep Bayern’s lead at one goal until former Liverpool forward Luis Díaz smashed in Bayern’s second off the underside of the bar.

Just as the game appeared to be out of reach for Aston Villa, who had struggled to create much going forward for most of the game, Unai Emery’s side showed some life late on. Summer signing João Gomes’s goal from the edge of the box gave them hope before Tammy Abraham nearly brought them back onto level terms, only to be denied by a fine save from 40-year-old Manuel Neuer.

Aston Villa are next in action in six days’ time when they take on Paris St-Germain in the Uefa Super Cup before kicking off their Premier League campaign at Brighton. Bayern have one more friendly to come against RB Leipzig before the DFL-Supercup against Borussia Dortmund. They then kick off the defence of their Bundesliga crown against Stuttgart at home on August 28.

Joao Palhinha, who is on the bench for Bayern Munich today, has been linked with a move to Aston Villa.

“Aston Villa are a top side with a very good coach. They have a large squad and will field plenty of talent. They have already played more friendlies than us, but for us it is a good challenge. It is a match in which we will learn a lot about our team’s fitness. It will be a good match for us.”

Aston Villa’s preparations for the new season continue out in Hong Kong as they take on Bundesliga champions Bayern Munich at the Kai Tak Stadium. These sides have met twice before; Aston Villa beat Bayern 1-0 to win the European Cup in 1981/82 before then meeting in the Champions League group stages during the 2024/25 season, with Jhon Duran’s long-range screamer giving Aston Villa a 1-0 win at Villa Park.

After losing two of their first three pre-season friendlies, Aston Villa have won their first two games on Asian soil and this game marks the end of their tour of the Far East. It has been a summer of significant change at Aston Villa thus far with some key members of their squad departing and some new faces arriving. Youri Tielemans has departed after Manchester United triggered his release clause and they then sold Morgan Rogers to Chelsea for a British transfer record £117m. Unai Emery’s side have brought in 20-year-old Swiss midfielder Johan Manzambi, Brazilian midfielder Joao Gomes and Alejandro Garnacho, with the deal for Garnacho being an initial loan. Manzambi is currently on the sidelines due to a knee injury.

Unai Emery’s side were dealt a blow during the World Cup as Belgian midfielder Amadou Onana picked up a serious knee injury. They were though boosted by the return of Boubacar Kamara after seven months out as he scored in the 3-1 friendly victory over BG Parthum. As one midfielder returned from injury in that game, another picked up a fresh injury as John McGinn suffered a knock to his knee. The likes of Ollie Watkins, Ezri Konsa and Emi Martinez are yet to return after their world Cup exploits. They begin their Premier League campaign with an away game on the south coast against Brighton on August 23 but will be involved in the Uefa Super Cup against PSG next week having lifted the Europa League last season.

Bayern Munich head into this season looking to win a third straight Bundesliga title and have won two of their three friendlies, losing once. They have added Moroccan attacker Ismael Saibari from PSV and German left-back Nathaniel Brown from Bundesliga rivals Eintracht Frankfurt to their ranks this summer. The likes of Harry Kane, Dayot Upamecano and Michael Olise are not available as they are still yet to return after World Cup duty but Vincent Kompany has confirmed that Bayern’s German stars are set to return.

After today, Bayern have one more friendly against RB Leipzig before the DFL-Supercup against Borussia Dortmund. They then kick off the defence of their Bundesliga crown against Stuttgart at home on August 28.

#2195. Football slots explained: How the games work and what RTP really means

Bookmaker sponsorship has been part of English football for two decades, and the sport has also become one of the most common themes in online slot games.

Gambling sponsorship remains highly visible across the English Football League, despite recent changes at Premier League level.

Sponsored content. This article was produced in paid partnership with Slotozilla and contains sponsored links. 18+ only.

The shirts of clubs competing in each EFL division (Championship, League One and League Two) are virtually identical to those of the previous campaign, yet bookmakers’ logos have disappeared from the front of Premier League strips for the first time since the early 2000s owing to a voluntary agreement reached back in April 2023. That agreement does, however, only apply to the front of shirts specifically in the Premier League, and no equivalent restriction applies to EFL clubs.

The EFL has not sought to introduce any such restrictions and does not intend to, with the division remaining under the Sky Bet brand until 2029. Even in the Premier League, clubs are still able to acquire high-visibility sponsorships from bookmakers on shirt sleeves and LED boards throughout the stadium.

As such, the closeness between the two industries over the last 20 years has simultaneously given rise to a distinct genre within online gaming.

There are now dozens of slot games featuring stadiums, footballs, referees, penalties and trophies. Fans who want to compare titles or check RTP figures can use slot information sites as well as operators’ own pages.

For example, Slotozilla is a gambling-focused portal that has compiled thousands of slots along with technical information and free demo access. Such databases allow you to understand how a game works before you spend any money.

A football slot isn’t necessarily a complex, modern game with dozens of bonuses. Essentially, it’s a standard slot machine where football serves as a striking visual theme. Instead of cherries and sevens on the reels, you’ll see footballs, whistles and trophies, whilst bonus rounds have names such as ‘penalty’ or ‘free-kick’.

The majority of footballing slots are reliant on recognisable imagery, ranging from fan and player celebrations to trophies, penalty shoot-outs and stadium floodlights. Notable titles such as Football Rules! or Hugo Goal were created with the intention of recreating the vibrant atmosphere surrounding major tournaments instead of simulating real fixtures.

Playtech’s Football Rules!, released in 2007, is a good example of this early approach. It has five reels, 25 paylines, a bonus round and Wild and Scatter symbols, with a default RTP of 97.06 percent, according to Playtech. Here, football is purely a visual layer on top of standard slot mechanics.

Goal!!! by Booming Games shows how the genre has since moved on. Instead of five reels and three rows, it uses a 4x4 grid with 16 paylines running horizontally, vertically and diagonally. Booming Games lists its default RTP as 94.92 percent, with medium volatility.

The difference between these two games clearly illustrates that the only thing they have in common is the theme. The maths, RTP range and payout structure within the genre vary just as much as in any other category of slots.

A football slot does not replicate any real match or tournament, although they are sometimes released to coincide with specific sporting events.

One example is Play’n GO’s Hugo Goal, which was released two weeks before the start of the FIFA 2018 World Cup in Russia. Co-founder and CEO Johan Thörnqvist explained in the company’s press release that the company launched the game at the perfect moment to offer entertainment to players between matches along with providing cross-selling opportunities for partners.

The pattern repeats with every major tournament. Ahead of the World Cup in the US, Canada and Mexico in 2026, studios once again released football-themed titles to coincide with the event. Among them was Maradona Golden Goal from Blueprint Gaming, a sequel to the game licensed for the 2022 World Cup.

However, the tournament only influences the design and release timing, not what happens on the reels. Hugo Goal, with its penalty shoot-out series, feels very much in the spirit of football; however, every ‘save’ is determined by a random number generator, regardless of what happens on the actual pitch.

Every slot has an information screen that sets out its rules, paylines, bonus features and RTP. On UK-licensed sites, verified adult players can usually also view a demo version showing how those features work before any money is staked.

For example, Slotozilla, an online gaming review platform, offers demo versions that use a virtual balance. You can’t win real money there, but you can’t lose any either. Features such as Wilds, Scatters, free spins and payline or ways systems are all explained there. You don’t need to place a real bet at all to get to know a football slot.

Users often get confused about the RTP (Return to Player) statistic, which can be seen next to the name of every slot. This feature is usually misunderstood, however, and represents the theoretical percentage of funds that the game’s mathematical model returns to players over a very long period.

Essentially, an RTP of 97.06% in Football Rules! does not mean that a player will bet £100 and get £97.06 back. A single short session may end quite differently, and the RTP metric is instead meaningful as a long-term statistical indicator and as a way of comparing the mathematical models of different games.

Demo mode, however, only explains the game itself. As soon as a UK player moves on to real-money play, the terms and conditions attached to deposits and free spins are just as important as the RTP. Reading the full terms before accepting or activating any offer, including wagering requirements, avoids surprises later.

Football has transformed into a popular genre within slots, which is interesting in the wider context of gambling and its relationship with the sport. Exactly how much space a bookmakers’ sponsor should occupy on footballing shirts worn by the masses is often a divisive topic across the British footballing sphere, while slot developers have recreated the game's atmosphere without using any club's shirt.

But there is one fundamental difference between a match and a slot game. In football, nothing is known in advance - and that’s precisely why people watch it. In slots, on the other hand, everything is calculated in advance; the RTP, mechanics and terms are all laid out before the first spin, and you can view them for free.

Gambling involves risk and no game guarantees a return. Set spending and time limits, and use GAMSTOP to self-exclude from all UK-licensed sites if you need to stop. Online slot stakes are capped at £5 per spin, or £2 for 18 to 24-year-olds. 18+ only. If gambling is affecting you or someone close to you, free and confidential support is available 24/7 from the National Gambling Helpline on 0808 8020 133.
