#2194. West Ham backed to strike double transfer deal with Man United & Blackburn Rovers

West Ham United have been urged to consider two major transfer moves in the upcoming January window, the first of the Scott Parker era at the London Stadium.

The Hammers entered their first Championship season in 14 years as the overriding title favourites, but in some ways, have flattered to deceive across the first 15 games of the campaign.

By no means has it been a total disaster for the East London club as they sit ninth, just six points behind leaders Birmingham City with plenty of encounters left to make up ground, but it was enough for the London Stadium hierarchy to install former midfielder, Parker, in place of Nuno Espírito Santo earlier this month.

He will have found out a lot about his new chargers in away encounters against Cardiff City and Blackburn Rovers before the international break, and it has since emerged that Parker has laid down the law in terms of potential transfer plans.

West Ham were tipped to win the division at a canter by many due to the vast experience and individual quality which runs through the squad in E20.

It recently emerged that Argentine forward, Taty Castellanos, who has scored just once this season, is attracting interest from River Plate, with a fresh transfer stance from Parker subsequently coming to the fore.

Alan Nixon reports that the Hammers boss is looking to recruit a specialist left-back and a new striker with a proven record at Championship level.

It remains to be seen who will feature on the club's eventual shortlist, but it has caused plenty of debate online as to who should front them in particular.

West Ham's current predicament at left-back is certainly intriguing, with Oliver Scarles featuring eight times so far this season ahead of Athletic Bilbao loanee, Adama Boiro, whilst 19-year-old, Ezra Mayers, was utilised on the flank in the 2-1 loss at Ewood Park, last time out.

As such, Football League World's West Ham United fan pundit, Ryan Horn, has urged the club to target Manchester United's Harry Amass, who is reportedly expected to leave Old Trafford in January after a lack of first-team minutes.

"My left-back option would be Harry Amass, the youngster from Manchester United. Obviously, he could be leaving for free next year," Horn told FLW.

"I feel like, if we could offer him a deal with first team football, him and Scarles going out for left-back, that'd be great.

"He's got a lot of raw talent. I think he'll be brilliant, and going for that younger approach, where we can have a player in a position for a few years to come with some resale value if a big club comes in for him.

"Because of the fact he's not being picked over Luke Shaw, I honestly feel for him. He should be playing more, and I think he'd be brilliant at West Ham under Parker," he added.

Joe Cole feels that ex-Chelsea teammate Scott Parker is the right man for the West Ham job

Horn also expressed his desire to see Blackburn's Andri Gudjohnsen in claret and blue, with the Icelandic striker one of just three Championship players already into double-figures this season.

"My striker pick would be Gudjohnsen from Blackburn.

"Again, young, scoring goals for fun. He works hard, has that sort of 'building for the future' feel, that spine where he could grow into his role a bit more."

Amass previously excelled in the Championship on loan at Sheffield Wednesday, before an injury in January significantly derailed his second stint in the division with Norwich City under Philippe Clement.

The 19-year-old's contract situation and desire for a fresh challenge means there is certainly scope for West Ham to secure a cut-price deal, but that would be far from the case with Gudjohnsen.

Blackburn tied the 24-year-old to a three-year contract last summer, and they could extend those terms until 2028, especially if a bidding war ensues over his services.

#2193. 3 managers Blackpool must target as club legend Ian Evatt departs after 12 months

Blackpool have had a terrible start to the season which has left them just above the relegation zone after 13 games. Club legend Ian Evatt had come in last season and their was plenty of positive signs coming into this campaign but they have now come through.

As a result, Evatt has departed the club, confirmed by Blackpool on Thursday afternoon. Evatt was a crucial player for Blackpool when they went from League One to the Premier League just over a decade ago, making over 250 appearances.

Steve Thompson will take charge of the team for this weekend’s game against Wigan Athletic, along with Stephen Crainey, Stephen Dobbie and Steve Banks. The Tangerines are on the lookout for a new head coach to take them up the table.

With that said, here are 3 managers that Blackpool must consider to replace Evatt…

Gary Rowett brings substantial Championship and League One experience and a proven ability to organise and stabilise sides quickly.

During long spells at Millwall and Birmingham he regularly extracted solid results from squads that were not the strongest in the division, often through compact defensive structures, aggressive pressing and pragmatic game management.

He is tactically flexible rather than wedded to one system and has a track record of improving results mid-season. For a Blackpool side currently near the bottom of League One, his experience of grinding out points and rebuilding confidence would be a clear asset.

Luke Williams’ standout achievement was guiding Notts County to promotion from the National League with a record points total and highly attractive, possession-dominant football. However, he was dismissed by Peterborough United in League One this season.

He builds sides that control games, press effectively and score freely, which could energise the Blackpool support and improve the squad’s technical confidence. He also has experience of the demands of League One and the Championship.

While his recent spells at Swansea and Peterborough were mixed, his proven ability to implement a clear, progressive style and maximise players in a promotion environment makes him a credible candidate to lift Blackpool up the table.

Matt Bloomfield’s strongest case rests on his excellent work at Wycombe, where he turned a mid-table League One side into one of the division’s most competitive teams, regularly challenging near the top while playing energetic, high-intensity football.

He is known for his player-centred approach, clear communication and ability to create a positive culture. Although his subsequent spells at Luton and Oxford were less successful, those were at Championship level.

In League One he has shown he can overachieve with limited resources, making him an attractive option for Blackpool’s current situation. With Blackpool struggling at the moment, his player-management skills could be crucial to get the club back on track.

#2192. 5 Ian Evatt replacements Blackpool must consider ASAP

Blackpool have confirmed the sacking of Ian Evatt following a year in charge at Bloomfield Road, with the Tangerines now intent on reviving their fortunes under fresh management and avoiding a battle against relegation from League One.

Evatt had led the side to a respectable 13th-placed finish during the previous campaign after taking over from Steve Bruce, though the former Bolton Wanderers boss would struggle to get going once again at Bloomfield Road this term.

Last Saturday's 1-0 defeat away to fellow strugglers AFC Wimbledon marked the sixth in just ten games for Blackpool, who now find themselves only positioned above Doncaster Rovers in 21st by virtue of goal difference at this moment in time.

Blackpool's key decision-makers eventually listened to intensified sack calls from supporters by relieving Evatt of his duties on Thursday afternoon, and whilst the relegation-threatened third-tier outfit have confirmed that Steve Thompson will be in charge for this weekend's clash with Wigan Athletic, the search for a replacement has already begun.

With that in mind, FLW has taken a close look at FIVE managers who Blackpool should be seriously considering as potential successors to Evatt.

One manager who should surely feature within Blackpool's thinking is Andy Woodman who, remarkably enough, currently finds himself out of work following a controversial EFL sacking for the ages from League One rivals Bromley.

Woodman had, of course, inspired the plucky Ravens' unprecedented rise into the EFL pyramid for the first time in their history before winning the League Two title against all odds last season, although matters both on and off the field in that particular corner of South London have not been plain-sailing as of late.

Nonetheless, it was a huge surprise when the 52-year-old's historic 289-match reign at Bromley came to an abrupt end last month, and one suspects he won't be sat around unattached for too long.

We rank all 24 League One stadiums by their ground's distance from the city or town, starting with the longest distance.

Woodman's resourceful ability to get the very best out of what was at his disposal with the Ravens certainly reads well, and he frequently elevated and identified talent with great success.

His strong body of work, albeit at a lower level, means that he should be entering the thinking of the Tangerines hierarchy in the coming days.

A previous specialist of the firefighting genre, Gary Rowett's knack in that field has faded in recent times and he may need to re-attempt his hand in a lower division.

As such, Rowett could represent the most outstanding realistic candidate available to Blackpool right now, with the former Derby County, Millwall and Stoke City boss having been linked with the vacancy at promotion hopefuls Bradford City prior to Michael Duff's appointment.

The last few years haven't reflected too kindly on Rowett, who led both Birmingham City and Leicester to relegations while setting Oxford United up for the same fate in the same season that he was in charge of the Foxes' demotion last time out.

Therefore, the wily 52-year-old may not score favourably in the consideration of Championship decision-makers and could instead drop to League One for the first time in his managerial career.

Previously, of course, Rowett has excelled in firing teams towards safety and, though much of his fresher work has been conducted on a short-term basis, there's no reason why he couldn't provide a stable, steady and longer-term presence with the Seasiders.

Matt Bloomfield, meanwhile, having reportedly held talks with Blackpool following the aforementioned Bruce's departure in October of last year, is only likely to re-emerge as a contender for the Tangerines' top job in the near future.

Bloomfield was ultimately unable to prevent Oxford's relegation to League One after succeeding Rowett, yet many believed his subsequent sacking from the Kassam Stadium to represent a surprise and even harsh call - albeit Aaron Ramsey has since vindicated the Yellows' change in direction.

Still aged just 42, Bloomfield's stock has taken a few hits and he's perhaps yet to recover from an ill-fated spell in charge of Luton Town, in which he fell narrowly short of ensuring survival on the 2024/25 second-tier term's final-day before failing to inspire a tangible promotion push in League One.

He has, however, previously excelled at this level, with Wycombe Wanderers having been situated just two points behind eventual-runaway champions Birmingham at the time of his departure to Luton in January 2025.

Bloomfield may now require a sustained spell back in League One leading a side with more modest expectations such as the Chairboys, where he was able to work his magic - and Blackpool can provide just that.

In contrast to Rowett and Bloomfield, Stuart Maynard lacks pedigree above League Two level, but that shouldn't rule the ex-Notts County and York City boss out of contention.

Indeed, at the age of 46 and with an obvious appetite to prove his managerial mettle higher up the pyramid, Maynard could be an ideal candidate for Blackpool in their efforts to not only survive, but begin laying the foundational blueprint for longer-term stability and success.

Much like Woodman at Bromley, Maynard's dismissal from York in the wake of promotion back to League Two following a decade-long exile as National League champions with no fewer than 108 points was scarcely believable. The difference, of course, is that Maynard did not even receive the opportunity to lead the Ministermen into the current campaign, and their loss could turn out to be Blackpool's gain.

Maynard has a solid body of work to boast of from his time at Notts County, where he failed to deliver promotion but did take the Magpies into the fourth-tier play-offs during their second season back at the level and ultimately put the foundations in place for Martin Paterson to go one better with the Meadow Lane outfit.

Blackpool fans, having sat through an overly-patient and slow-paced possession-based style under Evatt, would likely warm to a more dynamic, attack-oriented approach through Maynard, who would tick many boxes for the League One strugglers.

A more left-field option with greater risk but no shortage of potential upside, Ryan Mason remains available following his departure from West Bromwich Albion at the start of the year and represents an interesting candidates for lower-ranked EFL outfits to consider.

Mason was highly-thought-of during his time coaching Tottenham Hotspur and even taking occasional interim charge of the Lilywhites, which meant that many were surprised when the one-cap ex-England international failed to get going at West Brom.

He would take just nine wins from 27 matches across all competitions with the Baggies before being dismissed ahead of a relegation battle in the deeper stages of the previous campaign, with James Morrison going on to prove just how much his two predecessors in Mason and Eric Ramsey had underperformed at the Hawthorns.

It was, however, Mason's first senior, full-time gig, and it would be harsh to judge him entirely from that alone.

Blackpool fans, was it the right time to sack Ian Evatt? Who would be your number-one replacement choice, and what do you make of FLW's selections? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below!

Mason showed plenty of signs at West Brom that suggest he'd be a more-than-capable candidate somewhat lower down the ladder, with his progressive style, experience with young talent and connections within the game all representing healthy selling points for a club in Blackpool's position.

It's essentially a case of risk versus reward, and only Blackpool's key boardroom figures can decide whether it's a gamble worth taking amid the threat of relegation to League Two.

They do have a history of appointing older managers with their best years very much in the past, though, and there are worse options than going for a change of tact and rolling the dice on Mason.

The game was fairly even in the first half and looked set to head into the break level at 0-0 but Pedro’s header from Moises Caicedo’s corner put Chelsea in front.

The west London side then doubled their advantage seconds into the second half as Pedro scored his second, assisted by Pedro Neto.

Just minutes later, Chelsea had their third courtesy of a well-worked corner routine. Romeo Lavia’s delivery was met by Caicedo on the volley, with the Ecuadorian’s finish with the outside of the boot finding the bottom corner.

Chelsea have their third and their new set-piece coach Austin MacPhee will be delighted with that one. Lavia plays a flat delivery from a corner out on the left to Caicedo on the edge of the box. Caicedo hits it first time on the volley with the outside of his right foot and picks out the bottom right corner with perfection.

Chelsea go 2-0 up not even a minute into the second half. Neto, on the right side of the box, cuts onto his preferred left foot and crosses to the far post. Pedro gets on the end of it and, despite the Milan keeper getting a touch on the shot, the ball ends up in the far corner.

AC Milan have made a raft of changes at the break, which includes their new record signing Ramos, who has joined from PSG this summer. Former Chelsea man Nkunku is also on. Milan were back out from the dressing rooms a fair bit earlier than Chelsea were. It is rather farcical how long Chelsea took to re-emerge for the second half and they just saunter out as if nothing is up. Why on earth did the officials just allow Chelsea to take over 20 minutes for half-time?

Nearly 25 minutes after the first half concluded, we are finally back under way in Jakarta.

The first half in Jakarta is brought an end and Chelsea take a 1-0 lead into the break courtesy of Pedro’s goal on the stroke of half-time.

90 seconds into added time at the end of this first half, we have the first goal in Jakarta. Caicedo’s delivery from the right picks out Pedro, who has a free header inside the six-yard box and makes no mistake, picking out the corner. Pedro really should not have been afforded so much space in the six-yard box.

Palestra makes a run in behind the AC Milan defence and is found but De Winter gets back to put it behind for a corner...

Due to the water break, there will be three additional minutes at the end of this first half.

The first booking of the game goes the way of AC Milan as Pavlovic clears out Palestra. A deserved booking.

Neto and Pedro exchange a good one-two down Chelsea’s right to put the former racing down the right wing. Neto then tries to find Welbeck in the centre of the box but it is just behind him.

Then, moments later, Pedro spurns a great chance for Chelsea. Palestra’s cross from the left is just out of Palmer’s reach but falls straight to Pedro, who blazes over from 12 yards out. He was leaning back and should really be hitting the target.

The space opens up for Palmer to take on a shot from range but his effort is blocked by De Winter. Palmer has not managed to have that much of an effect on this game thus far.

Pavlovic drives forward from his defensive position through the Chelsea half. He gets all the way to the Chelsea box before shooting but he looked to lack conviction, firing off target.

We will pause for a drinks break, which unfortunately we became accustomed to during the World Cup. Palmer takes the chance to take a seat on the bench whilst taking on some fluids.

Neto takes a first-time shot from outside the box and looks to curl it into the far corner, forcing Torriani into a decent save diving down to his right.

That is good one-vs-one defending from Achemapong. Estupinan plays the ball into the path of Leao, who drives through the Chelsea half down AC Milan’s left. As Leao approaches the Chelsea box, Leao attempts to push it past Acheampong but the young Chelsea defender times his sliding challenge to perfection and puts it behind for a corner. Sanchez punches it out to the edge of the box, where Comotto takes it on his chest before volleying towards goal. The ball goes behind for another corner, which Chelsea just about scramble away.

Chelsea hit the post. Palestra drives forward for Chelsea down their left before the ball falls to Welbeck on the left side of the box. Welbeck’s effort hits the outside of the post. AC Milan keeper Torriani appeared though to always have it covered.

Chelsea gift the ball away inside their own half and Leao drives forward. The Portuguese forward moves into the Chelsea penalty area before getting his shot away but his effort is blocked.

A big, big chance for Chelsea to take the lead. A long ball is played forward in behind the AC Milan defence and Pedro gets the better of De Winter inside the Milan penalty area. It is then a one-on-one chance with Torriani but Pedro hits it straight at the Milan goalkeeper. Torriani should have come to claim that long ball and then Pedro should probably have buried that opportunity.

Chelsea win their third corner of the game as Palmer’s cross from the right takes a couple of deflections off AC Milan defender. Captain Caicedo has trotted over to take it out on the left. He sends it into a good area but a foul on the AC Milan keeper Torriani is given against Pedro.

Neto clips a free-kick into the AC Milan penalty area, which is chested down by Pedro. The Brazilian holds the ball up well inside the box before winning a corner. Chelsea play it short and Palmer crosses to the back post, where Palestra wins Chelsea another corner, this time out on the left.

Caicedo’s inswinging delivery is headed away at the front post.

Palestra, who has joined Chelsea this summer from Atalanta, is proving his versatility today; he has mostly operated for Chelsea at right-back so far this pre-season but he is starting at left-back in this game.

Camarda is played in behind the Chelsea defence and tries to get the better of Fofana inside the Chelsea penalty area but the ball just runs out of play across the byline before Camarda can square the ball.

We are under way in Jakarta, Indonesia, in Chelsea’s penultimate game over in the Far East. Before the game started, a huge AC Milan tifo was unveiled at one end of the ground.

It is 7pm in Jakarta yet the temperature is still up at 29 degrees with the humidity at nearly 70%. These conditions will be a proper test for both sides.

The teams emerge from the tunnel and we are just moments away from kick-off in Indonesia.

The trophies won by Franco Baresi, who has died at the age of 66, are their own monument to the AC Milan and Italy defender’s greatness. Six times a Serie A champion, five as captain, three European Cup-winning campaigns, 10 other winners’ medals and the distinction of being a member of Italy squads that finished in first, second and third places at World Cup tournaments demonstrate that his career, in a significantly more meritocratic era, stand alongside the giants even if the bias towards attacking virtuosos denied him the Ballon d’Or his talent deserved.

Yet they do not tell us why the grief, so tangible in Milan and beyond today, transcends tribal rivalry. It would be unfair to say that he transformed the art of Italian defending. Gaetano Scirea, the elegant Juventus sweeper who kept the 21-year-old Baresi confined to the bench when Italy won their third World Cup in 1982, had a surplus of natural ability, composure and intelligence. He did not need the raw-elbowed brutality of Claudio Gentile, heir to generations of Italian defenders influenced by the post-war influx of players brought up in the traditions of Argentina and Uruguay.

Chelsea’s pre-season continues out in the Far East as they take on AC Milan in the Gelora Bung Karno Stadium in Jakarta, Indonesia. Both clubs now have new managers, with Xabi Alonso taking over at Chelsea and former Manchester United boss Ruben Amorim now at the helm at AC Milan. These sides met in pre-season last year, with Chelsea winning 4-1 at Stamford Bridge. At that time, now-Manchester City boss Enzo Maresca was in charge whilst Max Allegri, who is now at Napoli, was leading AC Milan. The last time they met competitively was during the 2022/23 Champions League season, with Chelsea winning both games.

Chelsea lost 1-0 to AC Milan’s Serie A rivals Juventus on Wednesday in Hong Kong, in which Danny Welbeck made his first Chelsea appearance after joining from Brighton in the last week.

“We want to have a great team spirit and that is built on a daily basis with a great culture in the building,” Alonso said in his pre-match press conference. “That helps in the moment you have to compete. You feel better having that togetherness, trust, and the bonds between the players are so important to ultimately building a winning mentality, and a team that can compete anywhere. So the football side is so important, but the mental side is as important. And the strength of the team is the most important. We have been able to train and prepare [during pre-season] and I am very happy with the attitude and personality, we know that it is a very important part of this tour.”

It has been another busy week on the transfer front for Chelsea; not only has Welbeck arrived over the last week but so too have Jordan Henderson and Valentin Barco. Alonso’s men have two more games after this against Johor Darul Ta’zim and Real Sociedad before getting their Premier League campaign under way with a derby against Fulham at Craven Cottage.

AC Milan finished fifth in Serie A last season to earn a spot in the Europa League last season. Their big-money acquisition this summer as been Portuguese striker Goncalo Ramos from PSG. They have played two pre-season friendlies so far, which have both ended in draws; 2-2 against Celtic and then 1-1 against city rivals Inter. They kick off their Serie A campaign at Torino on August 23.

#2190. The 8 players set to miss Barnsley vs Sheffield Wednesday ft Mason Burstow

The November international break is over, but the first EFL matchup back takes place between two League One sides who weren't afforded a rest over the past week.

Barnsley host South Yorkshire rivals Sheffield Wednesday in a showdown between two sides at opposite ends of the table, and two fanbases who have contrasting feelings towards their higher-ups.

Sections of the Tykes fanbase are looking to stage a protest in front of the Sky cameras against their ownership and chairman, Neerav Parekh, with the club in a continued decline and facing the grim possibility of a first fourth-tier campaign in 47 years.

Their opponents know all about negative feelings towards their ownership, but this is a Wednesday side reborn under the stewardship of David Storch and on the hunt for an immediate promotion back to the Championship.

Dave Challinor's looking for his first win on the road as Wednesday boss, and three points could see them up to third before everyone else plays. Meanwhile, Barnsley need the points to avoid being cut adrift early in this relegation battle.

These eight players won't be available for either side in this big Friday night clash under the lights at Oakwell.

Barnsley didn't have a great time at Wigan Athletic last Saturday, losing 2-1 to one of their bottom four neighbours before action began.

What was made worse during the game was the realisation that their captain, Cameron McGeehan, would be suspended for Friday night's clash with Sheffield Wednesday after he picked up his fifth yellow card of the season midway through the first half.

#2189. 3 reasons why Bristol Rovers must sack Steve Evans

It was just a few short months ago that optimism was high and Bristol Rovers were widely being tipped for a hugely successful season. The Gas had ended the 2025-26 season unbeaten in nine matches and then started the new campaign by breaking the club record for consecutive wins at home. The optimism appeared well-placed.

Steve Evans was being lauded by Gasheads who were daring to dream. Iconic scenes of the gaffer in the Thatchers will be hard to replicate.

Now we’re approaching the festive period, and Gasheads are up in arms with large portions of the fanbase calling for Evans to go.

The Gas are once more on a miserable run of defeats in the league and have a manager either by accident or by nature creating division between himself and supporters, and even perhaps himself and his players.

Supporters have become increasingly frustrated by the manager's post-match comments in recent times, adding to the dismay at results on the pitch.

With so many Gasheads now calling for Evans to go, Football League World offers three reasons why now is the right time to act.

The last time Bristol Rovers were on such a deep losing streak — a run that saw the Gas fall to a club record 10 straight league defeats — the ownership decided to act and dismissed Darrell Clarke. A man widely seen as a great of the club following his promotion success years earlier. Rovers' latest defeat under Evans, going down 2-0 at home to Chesterfield, was their eighth loss in their last 10 matches in League Two.

When you compare the final 12 League matches for Darrell Clarke (one win, one draw, 10 losses) with Steve Evans' last 12 league matches to-date (two wins, one draw and nine losses), there is very little between them.

Both men, though, can have strong claims to be deserving of more time to put things right at the club.

Darrell Clarke was, and remains, a hero for Gasheads. Clarke is one of Bristol Rovers' most successful managers in history, having achieved a unique feat by gaining back-to-back promotions. And his connection with the fan base is unrivaled by any manager, perhaps since Paul Trollope.

Steve Evans has won nine promotions across his 32 years in management. And was able to turn the Pirates' fortunes around following his arrival after Clarke's dismissal last season, enjoying a run of eight straight league victories (their best run since the 1950’s) alongside breaking the club record for consecutive home wins. All of which means he may have plenty of credit in the bank with the current Rovers ownership, even if support among fans has all but extinguished.

As pressure increases on Bristol Rovers manager Steve Evans, we looked at six potential replacements the Gas should consider.

Evans caused a stir earlier this week when his post-Chesterfield comments left supporters confused and even concerned.

Following the Pirates’ defeat to Chesterfield, Evans took to the media to unleash a barrage of comments that left many Gasheads asking what on earth he was on about, and ultimately forced Evans into the most remarkable backtrack.

On Saturday, Evans told the media that he and his staff were being let down by “inconsistent and poor players”. Players, incidentally, who were predominantly recruited by Evans and his staff. Whilst perhaps unhelpful to the cause of pulling your squad together, these comments were not what got the heckles up amongst Rovers supporters. Instead, it was his remarks that many interpreted to be an allegation against a previous manager having been turning up to work at Bristol Rovers drunk when results were going against them.

Evans told local media after the match on Saturday: "We’re not doing our jobs, but we’re being let down by inconsistent players. Poor players, in my opinion."

He then went on to add: "I’m still first in. Half six, till six. I’m watching games midweek. I’m not like before, when they’re coming in at nine o’clock, leaving at two, being drunk half the week. That’s not me, yeah."

It sparked widespread condemnation of Evans and also speculation as to who he was referring to.

On Monday, Steve Evans was forced into an incredible backtrack from those comments as he spoke to Bristol Live in an attempt to quash the speculation. The under-pressure veteran manager told the local news outlet: "I get really annoyed when people speculate or make something up when I speak openly. I spoke about other managers waiting for payoffs, and yes, that happens in football because our jobs are hard to get, and you can be out of work for some time."

Adding: "Further, I spoke about some individuals having alcohol-related issues here. Some have made two plus two = 12, with social media alluding to I was referring to one particular manager, and nothing could be further from the truth. I was actually generalising about the behaviour of some managers when pressure mounts. I am indeed the opposite. Work and Bristol Rovers are what matter to me. I need a clear focus. However, that cannot be attributed to many other individuals."

Football League World has also spoken directly to players who were at Bristol Rovers during previous regimes, who strongly denied suggestions that any manager turned up drunk while they were playing at the club.

For a large swathe of supporters who were undecided whether Evans was the right man to guide the Gas out of their latest slump, this episode has tipped the scales.

In recent seasons, Bristol Rovers have acted in the weeks leading up to Christmas with four managerial changes happening in mid-December since 2018. And with the transfer market opening on January 1st, along with question marks around the strength of the squad Steve Evans has assembled, there's an argument for making that decision sooner.

Any prospective new manager will need matches and plenty of time on the training ground to assess the quality of this Bristol Rovers squad before setting in motion any recruitment plans. A new manager will need to identify the weak spots in the squad, starting perhaps with bringing in sufficient goalkeeper backup, and trying to address the lack of potency in the Gas attack.

Rovers have six matches scheduled for December, including a busy festive period. And with the Gas currently looking in the wrong direction on the League Two table, the pressure will be on any new manager from the outset.

#2188. Como vs Man Utd Preview: Team news, injuries, form and where to watch

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United travel to Italy on Wednesday, looking for their first win since September 10, when they face Como in the Champions League.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to build any momentum this season. Sunday’s 1-1 draw against Leeds extended their winless run to six matches in all competitions, leaving United with just seven points from their opening seven Premier League games.

The pressure on Carrick continues to grow. A win in Europe would offer some relief, but Como will not make life easy for Manchester United.

Manchester United began their Champions League campaign with a convincing 4-0 win over Sabah FK but lost 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in their next match. They have since drawn with Leeds, having also failed to beat Fulham and Tottenham in the Premier League.

Their last six matches across all competitions have produced three draws and three defeats. Michael Carrick is in pressure of his own and needs a response from his players, particularly with United sitting 13th in the Premier League and 13th in the Champions League standings.

Como have shown more promise. Cesc Fabregas’ side beat RB Leipzig 4-1 in Europe before losing 3-2 to Feyenoord. They returned to winning ways against Fiorentina, claiming a 3-1 victory that moved them up to fifth in Serie A, with 14 points from seven games.

Como have scored 11 goals and conceded eight across their last six matches in all competitions. United have struggled, with their only clean sheet of the season coming against Sabah FK.

United have several confirmed absentees ahead of the trip to Italy. Patrick Dorgu, Manuel Ugarte and Benjamin Sesko are ruled out, while Leny Yoro is suspended for the European fixture after picking a red against Atletico Madrid.

Lisandro Martinez is a major concern after coming off at half-time against Leeds with a thigh problem. Ayden Heaven, Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo are all doubtful for the game.

Como will be without Assane Diao, who is sidelined with a thigh injury. Moise Kean has a shin problem and is a doubt, while Trevoh Chalobah is also a fitness concern with a muscular issue.

The match takes place at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia on Wednesday, October 21, with kick-off at 5:45 pm BST.

UK viewers can watch the Champions League fixture live on TNT Sports 2 and HBO Max.

#2187. Rio Ferdinand disappointed Man Utd didn’t sign midfielder better than Elliot Anderson

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Rio Ferdinand has admitted he is disappointed that Manchester United failed to sign Ayyoub Bouaddi, with the highly-rated midfielder now making an impression at Manchester City.

Manchester City beat several rivals to secure Bouaddi’s signature over the summer in a deal worth £86 million. The Morocco international became the Premier League’s most expensive teenager, and Rio Ferdinand believes Manchester United missed out on a special talent.

The former United defender was particularly impressed by Bouaddi’s second-half cameo during City’s Champions League defeat to Paris Saint-Germain last week. His performance reminded Rio Ferdinand why he had been talking about the youngster throughout the previous year and during the summer transfer window.

Speaking on his YouTube channel, Ferdinand highlighted how Bouaddi was the highlight of the game against PSG and who they missed.

“He came on and showed PSG that they may have missed out on someone who was on their doorstep when he was playing in France,” Ferdinand said.

The former England international also confirmed that Manchester United had been interested in bringing the midfielder to Old Trafford, but the transfer fee was too expensive.

“Manchester United were in for him, 100%, but the fee was just too much,” he added.

Ferdinand was left frustrated by what he saw from the teenager, who turned 19 this month.

“Just that little cameo against PSG makes you go, ‘oh, I can’t believe we missed out on that. I can’t believe it.’ I’m so gutted, so gutted. He’s bad! He’s so calm under pressure.”

Rio Ferdinand suggested Bouaddi’s quality could soon make the midfield position in a packed Manchester City his regular role.

City already have Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez among their midfield options, but Ferdinand believes Bouaddi has the ability to stand out alongside them.

“They’ve got Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez in that midfield as well but this kid could be the best of the three, easily.”

#2186. Michael Carrick handed lifeline ahead of Chelsea trip

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick has come under immense pressure as a disjointed Manchester United sit just two places above the relegation places.

Despite the scrutiny and the news of a possible sacking, Michael Carrick has been given another chance at Manchester United.

Michael Carrick will remain in charge of Manchester United for their Premier League trip to Chelsea on Saturday after the club decided to give their under-pressure head coach another opportunity to turn things around.

United have gone eight games without a win in all competitions, with Sunday’s humiliating 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth leaving them 16th in the Premier League table, just one point above the relegation zone.

Carrick’s position looked increasingly uncertain after the Bournemouth loss. As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were at Carrington on Monday to discuss the situation.

It has been reported by Sky Sports that United have considered other options internally as well to replace Micahel Carrick but for the weekend, they are sticking with Michael Carrick.

Carrick took training on Tuesday and the decision means he has at least one more match to convince the club’s hierarchy that he can get United back on track.

Gary Neville admitted he was surprised that Carrick had survived the immediate threat of losing his job, although he welcomed the decision.

“I am surprised, but I am happy because I was absolutely certain that what I witnessed on Sunday would be the end of Michael,” Neville said in Sky Sports.

The former United captain explained that Carrick’s actions after the Bournemouth defeat had made him think the manager was preparing to say goodbye to the supporters.

“It looked like he was walking round at the end on his own with the players behind him, like it was almost a goodbye lap,” Neville added.

Fans continued singing the manager’s name after the final whistle, while the rest of the team drew boos.

Despite his surprise, Gary Neville hopes Micahel Carrick can use the extra time to get a response from his squad.

“I hope he can somehow get those players to start playing for him again because they haven’t played for him in the last few weeks,” he said.

The trip to Chelsea now becomes a crucial test for Micahel Carrick but United need a result and a far better performance to ease the pressure at Old Trafford.

#2185. Barnsley have a far bigger problem than Daniel Stendel right now

Daniel Stendel was the returning hero in the summer when he arrived back at Barnsley for a second spell as manager.

However, it has been anything but a success, with the club sitting in the relegation zone and in the midst of a long winless run.

The Reds are only above bottom-side Bromley, who have parted company with their manager, Andy Woodman, on goal difference. They have just two wins to their name, with three draws, and eight losses from their opening 13 matches of the league campaign.

Burton Albion, who shipped six in one match against Milton Keynes Dons, and Bromley, who saw Sheffield Wednesday score seven against them, are the only League One teams with worse defensive records than Stendel’s men.

At the other end of the pitch, it is no better. Barnsley have scored just 13 goals, with fellow relegation-zone side Peterborough United, AFC Wimbledon, and outliers Bradford City the only sides to have scored less.

Barnsley have claimed just two of the last 27 points available to them, and despite thumping wins against lower division sides in the FA Cup and EFL Trophy, have not won in league action since early September.

The statistics do not make for better reading either. The Tykes are 18th for xG conceded and have underperformed their xG created by more than five goals, according to FotMob.

The overall xG table places Barnsley 19th, four places higher than their current position, but that is still far below the hopes and aspirations of the Reds’ faithful.

Stendel was a promotion winning manager in his first stint at Oakwell, before the fickle nature of football reared its ugly head and the board sacked him following a winless run.

The German has enjoyed limited success since his 2019 departure, at Hearts in Scotland and Nancy in France, before returning to Hannover 96 to coach their reserve team.

Daniel Stendel's first vs second Barnsley spell (source: Transfermarkt)

The fixture list shows no mercy either, with Stendel having to prepare his side for a South Yorkshire derby against Dave Challinor and his Owls, before a trip to imperious Bradford City just days later.

The obvious answer might be to sack Stendel, after a nine-game winless run in League One.

But, that would only make the German a scapegoat for much deeper and more troubling problems. Barnsley have underinvested for years and failed to replace their departed stars.

A suitable replacement for Davis Keillor-Dunn, who is now at Wednesday on loan from Wrexham, was never sought, nor was it for David McGoldrick after his exit from the club more recently. Adam Phillips left in the summer for a fellow third tier side, with Cameron McGeehan adding a strong midfield option without adequately and directly replacing the skills that Phillips brought to the table.

Daniel Stendel is already under pressure at Barnsley after returning to the club in the summer

The Barnsley squad has seen repeated underinvestment by a board that has lost touch and damaged almost beyond repair its connection to the fanbase at the heart of the Tykes.

Removing Stendel and adding another name to the revolving door of head coaches and manager might be the easiest option, but it is far from being the solution to the gaping holes that have grown and been allowed to fester under sub-par direction.

#2184. Two days or two weeks – Michael Carrick future decision rolls into the international break

Manchester United will use the November international break to decide whether Michael Carrick stays on as head coach, after a three-game reprieve that delivered only one win.

According to The Guardian, Carrick came close to being sacked after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October.

Director of football Jason Wilcox and chief executive Omar Berrada discussed his position at the training ground the following day and agreed to give him three matches to turn things around before the break.

Those games brought a 2-0 defeat at Chelsea, a 3-2 win over Roma and a chaotic 4-4 draw with Aston Villa at Old Trafford on Sunday. United conceded eight goals across the three fixtures, and defensive frailty remains the biggest worry.

Wilcox is now leading a review of Carrick’s position. He will consult Berrada before making a recommendation to Sir Jim Ratcliffe, who oversees the club’s sporting operations. Talks are expected to begin on Monday and continue through the break.

The league table makes grim reading. United sit in the Premier League relegation zone with just one league win in their last 10 matches.

If he does win a stay of execution, results will need to follow. Manchester United cannot be languishing in 17th place.

Carrick remains defiant. Speaking to Sky Sports News after the Villa draw, he admitted his side “haven’t won enough games” but insisted he expects to move forward.

“I’m quite clear with what direction the club needs to go in, and it’s got to win games, said Carrick after the late Villa draw.

“I think there’s more than enough [to turn things around].”

If United do make a change, former Newcastle manager Eddie Howe is reported to be Wilcox’s preferred candidate. Howe has been out of work since leaving St James’ Park during pre-season, after four and a half years in charge.

In the meantime, there are no plans to strengthen Carrick’s coaching staff.

For now, Carrick is expected to be in charge for United’s next match, against Liverpool. Whether three games were enough to convince the hierarchy should become clear before then.

The trip to Anfield could be do or die for the decorated former United midfielder.

He’s not likely to survive a heavy defeat at the home of the club’s fierce rivals, but a galvanising win would surely buy him time and goodwill.

That’s if he makes it through the next fortnight before then. A decision or indication could be two days or two weeks away.

The only certainty at Old Trafford is uncertainty.

#2183. Monaco summit adds unnecessary drama to Michael Carrick situation

Graeme Hanna is the site lead of Read Rangers and also writes about West Ham for Read West Ham. A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Michael Carrick arrived at Carrington for Manchester United training as normal on Tuesday morning, while his bosses were preparing to decide his future in talks on the Mediterranean.

Chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox are flying to Monaco to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe, with the BBC reporting that the pair will recommend Carrick is sacked.The Guardian has indicated representatives of the Glazer family will also be involved.

The club insists the Monaco summit was pre-planned and is not solely about the head coach and while that may be true, it hardly matters.By allowing the meeting to become public knowledge, United have turned a straightforward football decision into unnecessary drama, compounding an already difficult situation.

What does is say about Man United in 2026 that a manager is given three games to secure his job, then left to train the squad during an international break while the executives are flying off to rule on his future? It’s farcical.

The latest chapter is another muddled process.Wilcox and Berrada met at Carrington the day after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October and agreed to give Carrick three more matches.A defeat at Chelsea, a win over Roma and a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa later, they appear to have reached the conclusion many expected a fortnight ago.

If the timeline holds, the decision will be made at Wednesday’s meeting, with an official announcement expected later in the week.United reportedly want to tell Carrick face to face, which means he could spend another day or two working under a cloud. In reality, it could be two days or two weeks.

The stakes could hardly be higher. United have won once in 10 league games, their worst-ever start to a Premier League season, and sit one point off the bottom in 18th place.

Should Carrick go, attention will turn quickly to his successor.ESPN reports that United States head coach Mauricio Pochettino is the favoured candidate, while Eddie Howe has been named as Wilcox’s preferred choice. Antonio Conte has also been mentioned.

Carrick deserves better than this.Whatever the outcome, a club that wants to be taken seriously should not be settling its manager’s future in the full glare of the media while he is still out on the training pitch.

#2182. BREAKING: Michael Carrick sacked as Manchester United prepare for Mauricio Pochettino

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have sacked Michael Carrick as head coach following a disastrous start to the 2026/27 campaign that has left the club in the Premier League relegation zone.

The former Manchester United midfielder was informed of the decision today after travelling to the Carrington after meeting chief executive Omar Berrada, director of football Jason Wilcox and co-owner Sir Jim Ratcliffe.

Michael Carrick initially took charge on an interim basis after Ruben Amorim was sacked last season. He had a great start in the interim role, guiding Manchester United to third place and securing a return to the Champions League after missing the last two seasons.

Michael Carrick sacked by MUFC. Top lad, got the team in the Champions League against expectations. Gave it his all, night and day. Protected others, never threw anyone under a bus. Worked in difficult circumstances. Leaves with head held high. I wish him and his staff the very…

His first full season at Old Trafford has been a completely different story.

Manchester United have managed just one Premier League victory from their opening 10 matches, collecting just eight points from five draws and four defeats. Their only win came against Ipswich Town in August, while a disastrous run of results has pushed the club down to 18th in the table.

Sunday’s chaotic late 4-4 draw against Aston Villa was another setback, with United failing to climb out of the relegation zone before the international break.

Manchester United have already identified Mauricio Pochettino as their preferred replacement, with reports indicating that an agreement in principle has been reached with the former Tottenham and Chelsea manager.

The Argentine is currently in charge of the United States national team but is understood to be interested in taking the Old Trafford job.

Pochettino’s contract reportedly includes a release clause worth around £7-7.5 million, which United would need to activate. Talks over personal terms are also understood to be advanced with Pochettino’s camp.

United are now preparing for another change in the dugout, with Pochettino expected to be handed the responsibility of rescuing their season.

#2181. Chelsea Monitor Jarrod Bowen And Crysencio Summerville

Chelsea’s wide-player search has moved back into focus after the Guardian reported that the club are monitoring West Ham pair Jarrod Bowen and Crysencio Summerville.

The update lands during a busy early market for Xabi Alonso’s incoming rebuild.

The Guardian’s latest transfer round-up states that Chelsea like both West Ham forwards, while an earlier report noted Chelsea and Manchester United had tracked Summerville.

That same earlier update also said Aston Villa, Chelsea and Everton had expressed interest in Bowen.

West Ham pulling away at Stamford Bridge 👀Jarrod Bowen stuns Chelsea early before Crysencio Summerville doubles the lead. pic.twitter.com/Ktwr4pKZGH

Alonso officially begins work on July 1, with Chelsea confirming he has signed a four-year contract to lead the men’s team.

The winger search is not just about adding more attacking names. It is about identifying profiles who can quickly give Chelsea sharper final-third decisions.

Bowen would offer immediate Premier League output, penalty-box timing and a direct threat from the right.

Summerville, meanwhile, fits the younger, explosive one-v-one profile that would sit neatly inside Alonso’s longer-term wing plan.

Read Chelsea has already tracked the separate Summerville angle, but this latest dual link sharpens the picture.

Chelsea appear to be testing two different solutions to the same problem: proven reliability or higher-upside acceleration.

With pre-season closing in, that is exactly the sort of market check Alonso needs before deciding how much of Chelsea’s attacking rebuild should lean on speed, touchline width and more ruthless wide output.

#2180. “Quite simply disgusting”: Gary Neville tears into Manchester United over Carrick sacking

Graeme Hanna is the site lead of Read Rangers and also writes about West Ham for Read West Ham. A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Gary Neville has launched a furious attack on Manchester United’s leadership over the way the club handled Michael Carrick’s dismissal.

United confirmed on Thursday that Carrick had been sacked, four days after the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa left them 18th in the Premier League with eight points from 10 games.

The club said it had reached the “extremely difficult conclusion that a change is necessary”.

Neville had already branded the process “classless” on The Overlap’s Stick to Football podcast before the announcement, pointing out that it was widely known Carrick was about to go while United were holding talks with potential replacements.

Speaking to Sky Sports after the decision was confirmed, the former United captain went even further.

“It’s just quite simply disgusting,” he said.

“That falls well below the standard of what you would expect in any other business, in any other industry. It would be constructive dismissal and you’d be getting sued.”

Neville took particular aim at the decision to hold the crucial meeting in Monaco, with chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox flying out to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe while Carrick was still taking training at Carrington.

“The optics of being in Monaco whilst the team, the staff, the people who are running the club on a day-to-day basis are in Manchester, it’s just not good and it reflects badly on the leadership,” he said.

It seems the only ones not to realise that are the trio above, but they aren’t stupid. How they explain it, or attempt to rationalise, it will be painful to listen to.

Neville also argued the club should have acted sooner. Looking back to the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth in October, he said United “effectively watched Michael Carrick resign on the pitch” as he waved goodbye to supporters at full time, only for the board to hand him three more games.

He summed up the episode as “indecision, lack of authority, just basically really poor leadership”.

It’s more than an episode, it’s an omnishambles that reflects the dysfunctional state of MUFC in its current guise.

United are now expected to move quickly for Mauricio Pochettino. The United States head coach is reported to have reached an agreement in principle with the club, which is prepared to pay his release clause.

First, they will need to wait until the USMNT completes its CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final double header against Haiti on Saturday and Wednesday.

Yes. Man United to wait until an international break to appoint an international manager…

For Neville, though, the bigger problem lies above the dugout and no manager can be the scapegoat for that massive issue.

#2179. BREAKING: Mauricio Pochettino agrees Manchester United deal to become new manager

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have confirmed an agreement with Mauricio Pochettino to become their next head coach, with the Argentine set to take charge after the international break.

The 54-year-old will replace Michael Carrick, who was dismissed on Thursday following United’s disastrous start to the Premier League season. Pochettino is set to sign a two-and-a-half-year contract that will keep him at Old Trafford until the summer of 2029.

The former Tottenham and Chelsea boss is currently managing the United States national team but is expected to fly straight to Manchester after completing his USA international games.

Pochettino will first oversee the USA’s CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final ties against Haiti on Saturday and next Tuesday. He is expected to arrive in Manchester on Wednesday before taking charge of the club on Thursday.

Pochettino’s first taste of action as a United manager will be against Liverpool at Anfield on November 22.

Pochettino brings considerable Premier League experience after previous spells with Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea. He has also managed Paris Saint-Germain before the USA job.

His appointment makes him Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013.

Carrick’s departure came less than six months after he signed a two-year contract. His second spell in charge ended after United managed just one win in their opening 10 league matches, leaving them in the relegation zone.

Carrick’s coaching staff, including Jonny Evans, Jonathan Woodgate, Steve Holland, Travis Binnion and goalkeeping coach Craig Mawson, have all left alongside Carrick.

#2178. Moises Caicedo And Kendry Paez Give Chelsea World Cup Signal

Ecuador’s late World Cup surge has given Chelsea a useful development signal through Moises Caicedo and Kendry Paez before Xabi Alonso’s first pre-season friendly on 28 July.

Chelsea confirmed that Caicedo and Paez reached the Round of 32 after Ecuador’s dramatic 2-1 win over Germany.

For Alonso, the contrast between the two Chelsea midfielders matters as much as the result.

Caicedo captained Ecuador through the pressure game, with We Ain’t Got No History highlighting the Chelsea midfielder’s leadership role in a win that rescued their tournament.

Paez, meanwhile, sits in a different phase of his rise.

That should not worry Chelsea. FIFA identified Paez as one of the tournament’s wonderkids, but Chelsea’s bigger win is that he can absorb elite-tournament preparation without carrying a defining role too soon.

Read Chelsea has already looked at how Alonso must handle Paez’s development curve, and this knockout place sharpens that argument.

Alonso inherits a midfield loaded with senior names, transfer noise and immediate pressure.

Caicedo gives Chelsea the proven pillar. Paez gives them the controlled upside.

Ecuador’s run now gives Chelsea both at once.

For Chelsea, that is the ideal succession picture: leadership today, patience for tomorrow.

#2177. Birmingham City's Powerhouse plans could change as club announce fan action

Birmingham City supporters have plenty of good reasons to be excited right now, with Chris Davies' side topping the Championship for the first time in many years, coupled with the well-documented plans set out by Knighthead.

Blues' American investors have completely changed the mood around St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park, and in a matter of years, such ambition is leading to a change of environment.

Having been mooted in April 2024, Birmingham unveiled the name and design of their planned 62,000-seater stadium a year ago this week.

The Powerhouse will pay tribute to the Second City's industrious past whilst being the centrepiece of the Sports Quarter regeneration project.

During the planning stages, Blues' owners have got many high-profile names on board regarding key elements such as transport, the creation of jobs and how the stadium will look on the outside and inside.

One figure constantly associated with the Sports Quarter development beyond Tom Wagner is Nick Smith, who became Birmingham's head of infrastructure in 2023.

Smith has issued several updates surrounding the planned development, which is set to be completed in the summer of 2031.

He, along with other Blues chiefs, have been in constant dialogue with supporters over the stadium move, as seen in April where he and Heatherwick Studio's Eliot Postma explained its scale at an event named 'Built by Us'.

With Thursday November 20th being the one-year anniversary of the Powerhouse's unveiling, Smith announced last week that another fan-led consultation will go live, titled 'Shaped by You'.

The concept will see Smith send out one question regarding the Powerhouse, to which Bluenoses have five days to respond, before feedback is detailed amongst relevant staff to help shape the experience into one which will be enjoyed by the entire fanbase.

Whilst the 12-chimney design has led to mixed reviews, it is one which will certainly be staying, but that doesn't mean there haven't been changes made in recent times to the overall Sports Quarter.

Indeed, it was recently confirmed that Birmingham are looking for a new patch of land to build training facilities which will house all of the club's men's, women's and academy sides under one roof, as well as a separate stadium for the women's team.

Birmingham City can seal their return to the Premier League by revisiting a move for Portsmouth star Adrian Segecic in January

The Sports Quarter is set to take up 135 acres of land, with reports detailing that the planned training facilities were set to take up over a half of such land, making it unfeasible.

This idea was heavily backed by Football League World's Birmingham City fan pundit, Jason Moore.

Compared to previous ownership regimes at St. Andrew's, few can fault the lengths at which Knighthead are going to in order to make supporters feel engaged with all that comes along with supporting Blues.

There have been several 'Open House' forums which have detailed goings-on on the pitch as well as administratively, and 'Shaped by You' isn't the only campaign which has been launched this month.

Blues fans, what needs to be considered the most ahead of the Powerhouse stadium move? Comment below!

Blues confirmed a new brand campaign ahead of the 3-0 win against Millwall on November 3rd titled 'Unreasonable', fronted by supporter and actor, Jayden Healey, who explained the concept.

"Is it unreasonable to believe that we can have a 62,000-seater stadium, be the biggest club in the world and play Champions League football? Yeah, that's the point of it. But, I believe we're going to get there," he said.

#2176. Man Utd impress in draw against PSG but injury worries mount

Michael Carrick said he had to be careful with Mason Mount after the England midfielder handed Manchester United an injury scare in their friendly draw with Paris St Germain.

Mount has had a torrid time with injuries since signing from Chelsea in a £55m-deal three years ago, starting only 25 Premier League games for United in that time.

The 27-year-old has been successfully deployed by Carrick in a central midfield role during pre-season, leading to speculation that United may not make another expected signing in that department.

But Mount played only 18 minutes of Saturday’s 1-1 draw against the European champions in Gothenburg before being replaced by Scotland international Tyler Fletcher.

“He [Mount] got kicked. We just wanted to be careful and look after him,” Carrick, the United head coach, told reporters in Sweden.

“We’ve been lucky with injuries so far, so we wanted to be careful.”

Post-match video footage showed Mount walking freely on to the United bus without the aid of a protective boot or knee support.

Michael Carrick told VG Mason Mount was taken off as a precaution. Looked OK leaving stadium. pic.twitter.com/Uo0fBtgpAl

United went behind within two minutes at the Ullevi Stadium when Liverpool target Ibrahim Mbaye punished slack marking to put PSG ahead.

Bryan Mbeumo fired United level after 32 minutes following good work from Amad Diallo, the Cameroon forward’s third goal in two games after his match-winning double against Atletico Madrid.

United meet Leeds in a Dublin friendly on Wednesday before coming up against AC Milan and their former manager Ruben Amorim next Saturday.

United open their Premier League campaign at Hull on August 22.

“It was good game. PSG is good so we had to keep building fitness. I am happy to have other [players] back and we have to keep going.”

“It’s good for me. I enjoyed it. We need to build fitness, we have a big season ahead.”

“It’s a good game PSG are tough to beat and we got a draw and now think about the next game [against Leeds in Dublin].”

“We have two more games against Leeds a and Milan. We need to have big season and hope to win a trophy.”

All over in Gothenburg and it’s been a good match for Michael Carrick’s team. They looked sharper and quicker than their win over Atletico Madrid last week (what the manager wanted) and things are moving in the right direction. Two things to worry about, however, were the early exits of Mason Mount and Tom Heaton with injuries. They’ll especially hope the Mount knock isn’t anything mid or long term.

Nearly done and it’s been a good run out for United. They’ve looked the better, sharper side and, if anything, deserve to win. In mitigation, this is United fourth pre-season game to PSG’s second...

Fernandes has one thing on his mind when he gets the ball 25 yards out in the centre of the park - a shot. He unleashes the attempt, destined for the bottom-left corner, forcing Longoni, the sub goalkeeper, into a good save.

Two chances for United in quick succession. A lovely ball from the middle of the park by Zirkzee finds Dalot on the right. He chips the ball to the back post where one of the Fletchers (sorry, I am not sure which) heads for goal. His attempt is blocked and the ball, ultimately, falls at the feet of Lacey whose shot is blazed high over the bar.

There have been plenty of good things for Carrick to take from this match. The passing, while not of the standard of a cut-and-thrust Premier League match, has been good for the time of year, and there have been enough flashes of brilliance/class/invention etc to suggest that pre-season is moving in the right direction.

Lovely bit of play by United. A great pass with the outside of the boot from the inside-left channel by Tielemans finds Fernandes who plays a sidefooted shot that goes just wide.

Tielemans’s first bit of brilliance from the Belgian there.

Both sides losing possession a fair bit at the moment. Strong pre-season vibes right now.

Maguire has had a good game. He’s played a number of long passes from the back and looked assured. This time he’s up in the PSG half and decides to have a pop - his shot testing Safanov, who saves well.

Had there been VAR Safanov might have had something to answer for...he may have beaten Zirkzee to the ball but he flew in the air and jurt the United man in the process.

Anwway, there’s no VAR (hurrah, for those of you who consider it a modern-day evil) and it doesn’t matter.

Chance for United to go ahead and it comes from two things. (i) A long, hopeful ball from Fernandes that looks for Zirkzee (ii) Safanov rushing off his line and into no-man’s land. The ball comes loose and Jack Fletcher, instead of having a go at goal, with the keeper on the ground and out of the game, passes to Lacey whose shot is wide.

Tyler Fletcher loses the ball near the United half and it, ultimately, results in a PSG corner. From the set piece United defend well and snuff out all hint of danger.

PSG seeing more of the ball at the moment. They work the ball to the right and Amass brings down Mbaye.

From the resulting free-kick, in the final third, the French side whip in the ball but Mee is up ot the challenge and come to collect well.

Santos, Mbeumo, Shaw, Amad, Lacey are off

Fernandes, Jack Feltcher, Zirkzee and Amass are on.

A raft of changes for PSG sees Marquinhos and João Neves, among others, come on. More like it, in terms of first-team ‘names’, on the pitch for the European Cup champions.

United on top in this half, as they were in the first.

The paces quickens and the game is much better for it. Mbuemo drives toward the PSG box, he cuts inside from the right before unleashing a shot that is bound for the back of the net before a fine one-handed save by Safanov. Credit, too, for Maguire whose long ball from the back found Mbuemo brilliantly. Promising...

Lacey tries to play in Dorgu on the left but he’s asking too much of his team-mate. Wonder when the expected raft of changes will take place?

Slow start to the second half. This is very much pre-season-friendly pace.

Having seen Mount limp off in the first half, there’s more worry at the start of the second and Heaton is force off with what looks like a hamstrung injury. He dashed off his line to clear a ball and in doing so has tweaked something. Mee replaces him.

Back under way Gothenburg - there have been a number of changes and will bring them to you once I’ve worked out who has come on and who they’ve replaced.

A world record that still stands...(this was back in 1995).

And that is it for the first 45 minutes. PSG took the early lead, but from that moment on United have been the better side and deservedly are level through Mbueno’s goal.

There will be one minute of added time at the end of what has been a good first half for Man Utd.

Amad has a go from outside the box forcing the save from Safanov. He’s such a live wire and one of the best in his position in the top flight. Him or Antony? No contest, strange it took United a while to work that one out.

Yet more good work from the underrated Amad. He again closes down well on the right before winning back possession and cutting the ball back for Lacey. To think he was starring for Sunderland in the Championship while Antony was starting for United...

United have been the better side since conceding earlier and, as if to illustrate my point, as I typed that an Mbuemo free-kick is well saved by Safanov. That was heading for the top-right bin.

Great work on the left by Dorgu and Shaw ends with the latter chipping the ball to the back post where Amad is lurking with intent. The Ivorian looks to head the ball home, but before he can get anything on it it looks as though he’s pushed in the back. United players are sure it’s a penalty but, more importantly, the referee doesn’t.

Amad does brilliantly, winning the ball off Koukaba in the final third, from there he scampers toward the box before playing in Mbuemo who takes one touch before firing in a shot into the bottom right corner of the net.

Free-kick for PSG on the right, near the final third. The ball comes in and it’s straight into the arms of Heaton in the United goal.

Kvaratskhelia, not seen much of him so far, runs into the box, but Amad does well to usher him back out.

The match is pedestrian at the moment, but as I type that, Dorgu, yep, him again, drives down the left and puts in a good cross that is well intercepted by Safanov in the PSG goal.

United continue to see more of the ball, Dorgu’s seeing more of it than most. But, no chances really fashioned, still, plenty of time to go.

Worry for United having to sub off Mount. It looks as though he took a knock to a foot and Carrick and co will hope it’s not a long-term injury. Mount, who’s had a good pre-season, has been replaced by Fletcher, son of Darren.

Foul on Mazraoui results in two things. The Mount has to come off, and, United have a free-kick on the edge of the PSG area.

From the set-piece Mbuemo has a go and it’s a regulation save from Safonov in the PSG goal.

Heaven loses possession in the middle of the park and from there it’s like the parting of the Red Sea as PSG have a three-on-one. Mbaye gets the shot in on goal but Andrey Santos covers back brilliantly and does enough to get something on the ball. He’s started both this match and pre-season well.

United are looking half decent on the ball. They’re passing it well and not afraid to switch things around.

A lovely long ball from Maguire finds Dorgo whose lovely first touch takes him into the box before a cut back is intercepted by a PSG man. Promising signs though for Carrick’s men.

United seeing more of the ball at the moment. They’re being patient but cannot fashion an opening and the ball is worked all the way back to Heaton in goal.

Needless to say this is being played at a slowish pace...no blood-and-guts challenges being put in.

Good work down the right sees Amad plays in Shay Lacey who crosses into the box. The ball find shte head of Mbuemo and the ball sails over the bar. Better from United.

United started slowly against Atletico and they’ve done the same here.

Bright start to say the least from the French side. They work the ball left and it’s an easy cross from Dro Fernandez for Mbaye to tap home for the very early lead.

They’re under way in Sweden. United are in their new vibrant red kit and PSG in their blue, red and white kit. Big fan of the French side’s kit, it harks back to the kit they had in the 1990s when the likes of George Weah and David Ginola were entertaining in the capital of France.

Man Utd: Heaton, Mazraoui, Maguire, Heaven, Shaw, Mount, Santos, Dorgu, Lacey, Amad, Mbeumo; Subs: Heath, Mee, Amass, Armer, Dalot, Yoro, Fernandes, J. Fletcher, T. Fletcher, Tielemans, Zirkzee

Paris St-Germain: Safonov, Pacho, Zabarnyi, Koukaba, Boly, Beraldo, Dro, Mayulu, Kvaratskhelia, Ayari, Mbaye; Subs: Marquinhos, Mendes, Lucea, Vitinha, Neves, El Nay, Idder, Drame, Bourdin, Ndjantou, Meite, Longoni, Chevalier

Manchester United continue their Sweden sojourn with match against Paris St-Germain in Gothenburg.

Clearly a clash against the back-to-back Champions League winners is a step up from their previous two pre-season friendlies (victories against Rosenborg and Atletico Madrid), but at the same time this is more a chance for players to get miles in the legs and see how new signings and possibly some young guns fit in.

One of those new signings is Youri Tielemans, and the Belgian international is in line to make his debut after his move from Aston Villa. Other team news is that Bruno Fernandes is back and ready to captain the team.

Michael Carrick has also been boosted by the returns of Matheus Cunha, Diogo Dalot and Noussair Mazraoui after their post-World Cup breaks.

Cunha, however, will not play a part in today’s match because of a “minor illness” according to the MUTV website. He is expected to link up with the squad in Ireland, where they face Leeds United at Croke Park, after the match.

This will be Man Utd’s first outing since the 2-1 win over Atletico, a performance and result that pleased Carrick.

“I enjoyed it, I thought there was a lot of good in things that we are looking to improve on,” the United manager said. “Building on what we did for the last seven months, I think there is a lot of good signs for us, it’s never going to be the exact stage we want to be at. But I thought there was really a lot of positives to take out of the game, individual, collectively as a team, senior players looking strong and fit, and the younger players doing really well.

“The spirit and the camaraderie is so important for the team, it’s great to see the boys getting on so naturally and enjoying each other’s company. Collectively we looked strong as a team, which is always the most important thing, and individual performances seeing the young players come on to have an impact.”

Kick-off is set for 4pm so stay here for the build up, team news and action.

#2175. Newcastle United slap massive €80m price tag on defender amid Manchester United interest

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United are currently in the Premier League relegation zone after winning just one of their opening 10 league matches.

Their defensive record has been shambolic, and Pochettino needs to fix it as soon as possible. They have conceded 19 goals in the Premier League this season, the third fewest after Crystal Palace and Ipswich Town.

Their defensive struggles could encourage the Old Trafford board to invest in a new centre-back, with Newcastle’s Malick Thiaw emerging as a potential option.

Amid their defensive struggles, Manchester United are among several clubs keeping tabs on Malick Thiaw as they look to address their problems under new manager, Mauricio Pochettino.

Newcastle United have set a massive € 80 million asking price for Malick Thiaw amid growing interest in the German international.

Several top European clubs have the 25-year-old centre-back on their radar after his impressive performances at St James’ Park. However, Newcastle are determined to not lose him on a cheap.

According to reports by Sky Germany, Matthias Jaissle’s side would want at least €80m for Thiaw, who remains under contract with the Magpies until 2029.

The German defender has also earned recognition from national team manager Jurgen Klopp, while Jaissle considers him an important part of his Newcastle side.

Newcastle signed the former Schalke defender from AC Milan in 2025 for a reported €35million.

Since arriving at St James’ Park, Thiaw has established himself as a key figure in the Magpies’ defence, making 64 appearances and contributing five goals and two assists.

His performances have attracted attention from several clubs, but Newcastle are in a strong position given his long-term contract.

Although a departure next summer has not been entirely ruled out, the Magpies are unlikely to entertain any approach that falls short of their €80million valuation.

Manchester United have a clear gap to fill in their defence line. Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez haven’t eased the nerves this season and the Red Devils need a change at the back. Malick Thiaw can be the answer but he isn’t going to be cheap cost United heavy on their pockets.

The big question isn’t the transfer value but whether he is the profile that Mauricio Pochettino needs when he rebuilds Manchester United.
