#2174. Pochettino begins Man Utd reign ahead of Liverpool showdown

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Mauricio Pochettino officially begins work as United head coach on Wednesday, just four days before his first match against Liverpool at Anfield.

The 54-year-old replaces Michael Carrick, who was dismissed last week after United won just one of their opening 10 Premier League matches, leaving them 18th in the table.

Pochettino arrives after completing his final assignment with the United States, who beat Haiti 2-0 on Tuesday to secure an 8-0 aggregate victory in their CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final.

The Argentine has signed a contract until the summer of 2029 and is expected to meet his new players and oversee his first training session on Thursday.

Speaking after his final match with the United States, Pochettino acknowledged the challenge awaiting him in Manchester.

“The new challenge is a big challenge, but at the same time it’s adrenaline you want to feel,” he said.

Several members of his coaching staff, including long-time assistant Jesus Perez, travelled to Manchester earlier this week to begin preparations. Pochettino will have limited time to work with his squad before Sunday’s Premier League meeting with Liverpool, which kicks off at 4.30pm GMT.

The former Tottenham, Chelsea and Paris Saint-Germain manager inherits a United side struggling for results after a difficult opening to the campaign. He previously guided Tottenham to the 2019 Champions League final and won the French league title during his spell with Paris Saint-Germain.

United’s next fixtures also include a Champions League trip to Sporting CP and a home Premier League match against Brentford. Pochettino’s immediate task is to improve United’s league position, beginning with a demanding first match at Anfield.

#2173. Chelsea World Cup Knockout Load Gives Xabi Alonso Pre-Season Problem

Chelsea’s World Cup contingent has moved from a group-stage watchlist into a genuine Xabi Alonso planning problem.

Chelsea’s own tournament update confirms that Enzo Fernandez, Mike Penders, Moises Caicedo, Kendry Paez, Malo Gusto and Jorrel Hato have reached the Round of 32.

England have also progressed after their final Group L win over Panama, which keeps Reece James and Trevoh Chalobah inside the wider knockout picture.

For supporters, that is a status marker. For Alonso, it is where summer pride starts to become pre-season compression.

The concern is not simply volume. It is where the minutes are landing.

Enzo has not missed a minute for Argentina, while Caicedo has remained central for Ecuador and captained them in the 2-1 win over Germany.

Those are not fringe workloads. They are the two midfield profiles Chelsea have built large parts of their control game around.

Alonso’s first Chelsea pre-season was always going to demand clarity through the middle of the pitch.

Instead, the players most central to that reset are still operating in knockout football.

Caicedo’s Ecuador role is the cleanest example. Read Chelsea has already assessed how his captaincy display against Germany strengthened his leadership case, but the next layer is more complicated.

Every additional knockout tie gives him more tournament rhythm, more authority and more fatigue.

Argentina’s route against Cape Verde keeps him on a glamorous stage at exactly the moment Chelsea need to settle his role, mood and tactical responsibilities under Alonso.

If Madrid-related noise continues around him, the club cannot afford a sluggish July handover.

Reece James started England’s first two matches before a hamstring issue ruled him out of the Panama game, according to the Guardian.

England still topped the group and moved into a last-32 tie with DR Congo, but James’ situation is exactly the kind of detail Chelsea’s medical team will monitor closely.

Chelsea have already had to plan around a large World Cup group.

The broader issue was clear in Read Chelsea’s earlier pre-season workload piece, but the knockout stage sharpens it because the returns will now be staggered.

Enzo brings a maximum group-stage load. Caicedo brings captaincy responsibility and heavy Ecuador minutes.

James brings knockout qualification mixed with another hamstring-management question.

Gusto and Hato remain tied to France and the Netherlands, while Penders and Paez are still absorbing tournament environments before club reintegration.

That is a spine issue, not a fringe issue.

Alonso needs automatisms in possession, spacing around the double pivot, clearer full-back behaviour and pressing triggers from midfield.

Those details do not bed in properly when key players arrive in drips after a major summer tournament.

Players returning from knockout football can bring competitive sharpness that closed-door friendlies cannot replicate.

Caicedo captaining Ecuador through pressure games matters. Enzo playing high-leverage minutes for Argentina matters.

Gusto and Hato absorbing tournament detail also helps Chelsea’s defensive reset.

But Chelsea cannot treat every tournament minute as clean preparation.

Alonso must decide who returns straight into tactical work, who needs a genuine pause and who can handle August without creating an avoidable injury bill by September.

That is where the James situation feels central.

If Chelsea’s captain carries a World Cup hamstring warning, the club’s right-sided structure becomes more than a selection debate.

It becomes an availability question that touches Gusto, the wider defensive rebuild and the need for control from midfield.

Read Chelsea has already looked at James’ captaincy test under Alonso, and the injury layer now makes that test sharper.

This is the hidden cost of a strong international footprint.

Chelsea want players good enough to survive deep into tournaments.

Alonso now has to make sure that status does not leave his first league weeks short of the very control those players are meant to provide.

#2172. ‘Sometimes to love is to let go’, former Man Utd midfielder announces retirement

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Former Manchester United midfielder Juan Mata has announced his retirement from professional football at the age of 38, bringing an end to a beautiful career of around 20 years.

The Spaniard went on to win one of the biggest trophies in professional football. The former Manchester United midfielder confirmed his decision on Wednesday after ending his playing career with Australian side Melbourne Victory.

Mata enjoyed a memorable spell at Manchester United after arriving from Chelsea in January 2014. He spent more than eight years at Old Trafford, making 285 appearances, scoring 51 goals and winning four trophies.

His first trophy with Manchester United came in the 2016 FA Cup final against Crystal Palace. He also helped the club win the Community Shield, League Cup and Europa League during the 2016/17 campaign.

Before moving to Manchester, Juan Mata had established himself as one of Chelsea’s most creative players. He won the club’s Player of the Year award in both of his first two full seasons at Stamford Bridge before eventually joining United as David Moyes’ marquee signing.

Mata probably had the biggest moments of his career in the Spain shirt. He earned 41 international caps and scored 10 goals, becoming part of the most talented Spanish team in history. He went on to win the 2010 FIFA World Cup and Euro 2012 with Spain while etching his name in Spanish football history with a goal in Spain’s 4-0 victory over Italy in the European Championship final.

After leaving Manchester United in 2022, the midfielder continued his career with Galatasaray, Vissel Kobe and Western Sydney Wanderers before joining Melbourne Victory in September 2025.

His final season in Australia was an impressive one. Mata registered five goals and 13 assists, won the Johnny Warren Medal and earned a place in the A-League Team of the Season.

Sometimes, to love… is to let go. THANKS FOOTBALL. ⚽️❤️♾️A veces, amar… es dejar ir. GRACIAS FÚTBOL. ⚽️❤️♾️ pic.twitter.com/v7otd6AyOq

Reflecting on his decision, Mata spoke about the reason that kept him playing for so many years.

“I was able to keep going for so many years because of the sheer joy of playing,” he said.

He also made it clear that retirement would not mean walking away from football altogether.

“Football will never leave my life. There are so many ways to continue loving the game.”

Manchester United also paid tribute to their former midfielder, writing on social media: “Great player, better person. Thank you for representing our colours with class, Juan.”

The persona he had, the brilliant assists, goals like that one on Anfield and more importantly the joy he brough to United fans in a difficult time at the club.
